| SH-18 | **Welcome splash's 4 animated stat counters replaced with a static capability strip.** John's live feedback: "tasks in progress"/"agents working"/"deliverables completed"/"$372M in spend analyzed" weren't worth showing — read as internal dashboard activity, not a reason to care, and the last one was leftover procurement-specific language contradicting the confirmed general-enterprise platform positioning (`project-deepbench` memory, S-PM-05, 2026-06-23). The real problem, in John's words: people don't understand what the agents actually do. Replaced entirely with "AUTOMATE: Market Intelligence · Data Analysis · Project Management" — three real, already-shipped capability domains (Marcus/Priya/Owen's Market Intelligence screen; Nadia Farouk's Data Expert role/`data-analysis` capability; Michelle Manning's Project Manager role), confirmed against `src/data/agents.js` before speccing, not fabricated marketing copy. Deliberately static, no live Supabase wiring — John's explicit call. Fix, 1 file, 1 task: removed the `countersRan` ref/`useEffect`, `animateCounter()`, `StatBlock`, and 4 now-dead styles (`statBlock`/`statNumber`/`statDesc`/`pulseDot`); added `capabilityRow`/`capabilityItem` styles reusing existing `pulseLabel`/`divider`. Node test 24/24 PASS (confirms new copy present, old copy/dead code fully removed, existing headline/CTA/dismiss logic untouched), build clean. **Independently re-verified by the design session, not accepted from the coding report alone:** own isolated dev server (Vite on port 5199, this session's own worktree) confirmed via the browser preview tooling — cleared `sessionStorage` and reloaded to force a fresh splash render: "AUTOMATE"/"Market Intelligence"/"Data Analysis"/"Project Management" render immediately with no counting delay, no `$` figures or old copy anywhere, headline/subhead/CTA pixel-identical, both the CTA and "×" close button correctly dismiss the splash, zero console errors, `npm run build` clean. Ran in its own git worktree (`session/splash-metrics`), zero collisions. Kickoff: `docs/kickoffs/v6.1.12-S-SPLASH-02-capability-strip.md`. | ✅ Done | S-SPLASH-02 (v6.1.12) |

| SH-11 | Restructure serverless API layer. **Fully resolved 2026-07-02 (`S-CAPABILITY-EXEC-02`):** superseded 2026-07-01 by `AA-76` (Generic Capability Executor) — the real fix wasn't consolidating routes one merge at a time, it was that capabilities shouldn't be routes at all. Both retrofits now done: `channel-intelligence.js` → `execute.js` (`S-CAPABILITY-EXEC-01`, serverless count 12→13→12), `quality-gate.js` → `execute.js` (`S-CAPABILITY-EXEC-02`, serverless count 12→11). | AI-43 | `sendRequest()`'s `latency_ms` was wrong for every capability routed through the post-v6.0.0 `execute.js` loop — its own clock restarted after the loop's `precomputed_turn` model call already happened, silently excluding the actual model-call time from the logged number. Found via a `quality-gate` smoke test (`latency_ms: 49`, physically impossible), reconfirmed live 2026-07-07 on Priya's real 27.5s hypothesis-test call (wrapper row logged `116`). Fix: `execute.js`'s already-correct `turnStart` threaded into `sendRequest()` as a new optional `turn_started_at` param — every caller that omits it (`api/plan.js`, `confirmation.js`) falls back to `Date.now()`, byte-identical to before. Independently re-verified by the design session (not accepted from the coding report alone): `git show` confirmed the exact 2-file diff; ran the Node test directly (both mocked cases PASS); a live pre/post-deploy comparison via direct Supabase query showed the same call shape jump from `latency_ms: 54` (pre-fix) to a correct `1739` (post-fix, matching its `agent-turn` sibling's `1675ms` within guardrails overhead) — and a second, independently-noticed example (Michelle's `project-manager` call, `8895` wrapper vs `8126` real turn) confirmed the fix generalizes beyond the one capability the coding session tested. | ✅ Done | S-ARCH-LATENCY-FIX-01 (79128b5) |

Verified live 2026-07-02: probing `api/rag-query.js` with `agent_id: "apple-cso-data-room"` post-migration returns `{"context":"","chunks":[],"matchCount":0}` — the endpoint remains public (by design, for personal-training content) but the Data Room data it used to be able to leak is no longer physically reachable through it. | ✅ Done | S-LIBRARIAN-03 (c3b0d72) |

| AI-41 | **Manual QA regression, `S-CAPABILITY-EXEC-01` (2026-07-02):** `channel-intelligence.js`'s Marcus calls used to log with specific, correctly-mapped `ai_type` values (`ci_routing`/`ci_answer`) via its own now-deleted `logActivity()` function. Retrofitted through `execute.js` → `sendRequest()`, they logged via `sendRequest()`'s generic Step 4 write instead, which hardcoded the literal `ai_type: 'request-receivable'` — losing the `ci_routing`/`ci_answer` distinction entirely. Root cause: `sendRequest()` already receives `capability_slug` as a parameter but never used it in the log write. **Fixed `S-CAPABILITY-EXEC-02` (2026-07-02):** Step 4 now logs `ai_type: capability_slug`, restoring per-capability AI Audit attribution platform-wide (not just for `channel-intelligence`/`quality-gate` — every `sendRequest()` caller, including `plan.js`, benefits). One new `AI_TYPE_TO_SERVICE` entry added (`'project-manager': 'task-planning'`) for `plan.js`'s default `capability_slug`; `channel-intelligence`/`quality-gate` needed no new entry since their `capability_slug` values already match their `SERVICE_CATALOG` slugs exactly via the existing `|| e.type` fallback. Live-verified against the deployed dev URL (not just Node test): a fresh call through `execute.js` produced a real `ai_activity_log` row with `ai_type: 'quality-gate'`, Quality Gate's By-Service call count increased, Channel Intelligence's was unaffected. 6/6 confirmable Manual QA items PASS (verified directly via Supabase + live dev URL calls); Vercel function log check (item 7) not independently verifiable this session, accepted on indirect evidence (clean 200 responses, no errors) per John's go-ahead. | ✅ Done | S-CAPABILITY-EXEC-02 (4ba5bc4) |
| AI-36 | Pattern type classification — every pattern in the registry carries a `patternType` field: `structural` (scaffolding around Claude — changes what Claude sees or when it is called, not how it reasons: RAG, Streaming, Structured Output, Embeddings, Browser Automation) or `reasoning` (changes how Claude thinks — same prompt produces meaningfully different output: ReAct, Tool Use, Prompt Chaining, Reflection, Multi-Agent Debate, Chain-of-Verification). Design requirement: (1) AI Audit By Pattern section visually distinguishes the two types — separate groupings or a type badge per row; (2) counts, cost, and availability (active/partial/roadmap) tracked and displayed per type group; (3) AiBadge labels and tooltips app-wide identify pattern type so users can see whether an element is using structural or reasoning architecture; (4) Platform Roadmap section groups roadmap patterns by type. Registry field: `patternType: 'structural' | 'reasoning'`. All 20 patterns in PATTERN_CATALOG must be classified before S-PAT-REGISTRY-01 coding begins. Depends on AI-35. Classification locked 2026-06-16: Structural (9) = RAG, Streaming, Structured Output, Embeddings, Browser Automation, Guardrails, Parallelization, HyDE, Adaptive RAG. Reasoning (11) = ReAct, Tool Use, Prompt Chaining, Reflection, HITL, Agent Orchestration, Few-Shot Prompting, LLM-as-Judge, Multi-Agent Debate, Chain-of-Verification, Episodic Memory. | ✅ Done | S-AI-PATTERN-TYPE-01 (22c9d2e) + patch (26ff072) |
| AI-39 | The Proofreader (`quality-gate`/Owen) had zero live callers anywhere on the platform since `S-APPLE-02c` built it — `S-CAPABILITY-EXEC-02` correctly deleted its standalone route (with the retry-on-block logic inside it) when generalizing every capability onto `execute.js` (`ARCHITECTURE.md` §19b), but the retry mechanism itself was never rebuilt anywhere. Confirmed live during `01b`'s QA: Marcus's self-flag alone missed the curated EMEA co-op demo case 4/4 times. **Closed by `S-MARKET-INTEL-01c`:** `runQaWithQualityGate()` in `MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx` rebuilds the retry-once-on-block mechanism as a Layer 2 orchestration (two ordinary `execute.js` calls — Marcus, then Owen), and computes `needs_review` as Marcus's self-flag OR Owen's independent `eval.result==='revise'`, per the locked design doc §5.2 rule. Also fixed a real gap in the original design: `quality-gate`'s `task_context` never included the original question, which `qg-review-intent`'s `responsiveness` scoring dimension requires — now included. **Root cause found during the coding session's own Category L test, not a defect in the shipped screen code:** `qg-review-intent`'s Supabase `max_tokens` was `600`, truncating the `eval` object on every real call before it could complete (same failure shape as `01b`'s `hyp-generation-intent` truncation bug) — raised to `1500`. Node test (categories A/B/C/G, including a pure-function retry simulation covering block-then-pass and double-block per `STANDARDS.md` Section 4's retry-logic-test requirements) and Category L (3 live calls against the real deployed capability) both PASS. **Independently re-verified by the design session directly against the live deployed dev URL** (not accepted from the coding report alone): a real `ci-answer-intent` answer that Marcus self-flagged `needs_review:false` was blocked by Owen for `hallucinated_internal_data`; the retry-once regenerated the answer, passed guardrail, but `eval.result:'revise'` still correctly flagged it — full end-to-end proof of the two-layer OR-gate and retry-once mechanism working exactly as designed, live, on real production data (not a synthetic test fixture). Kickoff: `docs/kickoffs/v6.0.20-S-MARKET-INTEL-01c-proofreader-pipeline-log.md`. | ✅ Done | S-MARKET-INTEL-01c-design/S-MARKET-INTEL-01c (v6.0.20, 7952395/2282513) |
| AI-50 | AI Audit's "By Pattern" and MI screen's Agent Routing subline both rolled up cost/patterns from `SERVICE_CATALOG`'s static per-*service* tags, not real per-call data — confirmed ~3x cost overcount (\$62.10 vs \$21.03 Total Cost). Fixed across 4 sessions same day (2026-07-14): **`AI-50a`** (v6.2.23, `6879225`) threaded each Intent Skill Profile's real `technical_services` through `db-assembly.js`→`ai-enrichment.js`→`request-receivable.js` into `ai_activity_log.patterns_used`, no schema change. **`AI-50a2`** (v6.2.26, `23e4ba0`) extended the same merge fix to `execute.js`'s `logAgentTurn()`, a second independent write site `AI-50a` missed — pure-delegation intents (`ci-answer-display-intent`, `hyp-hypothesis-test-display-intent`) never reached `sendRequest()` and always logged mechanical-only data until this fix. **`AI-50b`** (Supabase content only, no version) populated `technical_services` for the 5 remaining empty Intent Skill Profiles and gated `hyp-knowledge`'s RAG to only the 2 intents that use it. **`AI-50c`** (v6.2.28, `8910857`) rewrote `useAIActivity.js`'s `byPattern` as a testable `computeByPattern()` (real per-call pattern attribution, cost split evenly across genuinely-simultaneous patterns per call) and `MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`'s Agent Routing subline to read real `evt.data.patterns_used`; fixed 2 catalog gaps (`'reflection'`→`'reflect'` slug rename, added `'intelligent-synthesis'`). Each session live-verified against real production data/deployment, not just Node tests — `AI-50c`'s own check fed 200 real rows through the shipped `computeByPattern()` and confirmed bucket-sum cost reconciles exactly (to the fraction of a cent) to the sum of entries with real pattern data, closing the overcount bug structurally. Known deliberate scope boundary: `execute.js`'s `resumeCapability()` checkpoint/resume path still falls back to mechanical-only pattern detection — not fixed by this effort. Full narrative for all 4 sessions: `docs/SESSIONS.md`. | ✅ Done | S-AI-50a-design/S-AI-50a (v6.2.23, `6879225`) ✅ — S-AI-50a2 (v6.2.26, `23e4ba0`) ✅ — S-AI-50b ✅ — S-AI-50c (v6.2.28, `8910857`) ✅ |
| AI-51 | **Found 2026-07-14, John reviewing the live AI Audit screen right after `AI-50c` shipped.** Two issues: By Pattern's bucket sum ran ~\$4-6 short of Total Cost (root-caused as mostly-expected content-coverage backlog, matches `AI-50c`'s own design) — but investigating it surfaced a genuine, quantified double-count bug: `execute.js`'s `logAgentTurn()` writes a row for every turn, `sendRequest()` writes a second row for the same turn when terminal, both carrying identical tokens/`patterns_used` — both were being priced and both counted toward `computeByPattern()`'s per-pattern totals. Live-quantified: 1,107 paired `agent-turn` rows, \$5.58 double-counted out of \$22.82 Total Cost (~24% inflation). A blanket "zero all agent-turn cost" fix would be wrong — pure delegating turns have no paired wrapper row and are the sole record of that spend. Fix reused `useAgents.js`'s already-proven-live `S-MI-20` pairing check (`CAPABILITY_WRAPPER_TYPES`/`PAIR_WINDOW_MS`, built for latency dedup) applied to cost + pattern-count instead — moved to `useAIActivity.js` as the canonical shared module, new exported `pairedAgentTurnIds()`, wired into both `hydrateFromSupabase()` and `useAgents.js`'s `buildActivitySummary()`. Also fixed `AIActivityPanel.jsx`'s "Services Active" stat hardcoding a stale `/14` denominator (`SERVICE_CATALOG` has 32 real entries today) via a new `servicesCatalogTotal` field. 3 files, Node test 5/5 PASS (coding session also caught and fixed a real gap beyond the kickoff's literal spec: `useAgentActivitySummary()`'s Supabase `.select()` was missing `id`, which would have made every row share `id: undefined` and falsely pair the whole batch). **Design session's own live verification against real production data:** replicated the shipped pairing/cost logic directly in SQL against all 6,868 real rows — confirmed Total Cost drops from \$23.68 to \$18.53 (removing \$5.15 across 1,027 paired rows), while a genuine pure-delegation `agent-turn` row with no paired wrapper (real tokens, id 9746) correctly retains its real cost, proving both directions of the fix. Kickoff: `docs/kickoffs/v6.2.34-AI-51-agent-turn-double-count-and-service-count-fix.md`. | ✅ Done | S-AI-51-design/S-AI-51 (v6.2.34, `b828422`) ✅ |
| AI-49 | **Found 2026-07-13 while pulling live data for `AI-48`'s design mock:** every row in the most recent 500-row `ai_activity_log` window had `cost_usd = 0`/`null`, including real LLM calls with real token counts. **Root-caused and fixed 2026-07-14 (`S-AA-181-design`/`S-AA-181`, tracked as `AA-183` — see that row in the AGENT ARCHITECTURE — AA section below for the ID-collision note):** every server-side write site logs `input_tokens`/`output_tokens` but never computes `cost_usd`; fixed at read time via a shared `computeCallCost()` using real split input/output Anthropic pricing, no schema change. Full detail: `AA-183` (below). | ✅ Done | S-AA-181-design/S-AA-181 (v6.2.19, 042a5ba) |
| AI-47 | Two distinct `ai_activity_log` token-write bugs, root-caused 2026-07-08 while trying to root-cause `AA-151` (a shipped `max_tokens` fix hadn't moved the needle, and there was no real token data to check the theory against). Bug 1: `execute.js`'s `logAgentTurn()` (the delegation-loop's per-turn write) had no token parameter in its signature at all — `turn.usage` was available at its call site and simply never passed through. Bug 2: `request-receivable.js`'s `sendRequest()` STEP 4 wrote a number but mislabeled it — stuffed the combined input+output total into `input_tokens`, never wrote `output_tokens`. Fixed both (2 files), Node test 2/2, build clean. Live-verified directly by the design session against the deployed dev URL (not accepted from the coding report alone) — confirmed real, correctly-labeled `input_tokens`/`output_tokens` now populate on fresh calls through both write sites, including `agent-selection-intent`'s own turns, which is what actually unblocked `AA-151`'s real diagnosis (see that row: budget theory subsequently ruled out by the real data this fix produced). | ✅ Done | S-ARCH-AI-ACTIVITY-TOKENS-01 (v6.1.33) |
| LOG-12 | **Found + fixed 2026-07-16 live-testing a real Channel Intelligence question (John: "highest priority for the AI audit log").** AI Audit's `byService` grouped strictly by raw `ai_type`, so every non-terminal delegation-loop turn (`ai_type: 'agent-turn'`) landed in the generic "Agent Turn" bucket instead of its real capability — measured live, 4 of Owen's 5 real Quality Gate review calls were invisible under "Quality Gate." | LOG-14 | **Found + fixed 2026-07-16, John's explicit direction: catalogs must be self-maintaining.** `SERVICE_CATALOG`/`PATTERN_CATALOG` are static hand-maintained lists — any real service/pattern slug not yet catalogued was silently invisible in By-Service/By-Pattern (not miscategorized — absent). Concrete instance: `guardrails-check` had zero `SERVICE_CATALOG` entry and zero `AI_TYPE_TO_SERVICE` mapping (real Alex/Michelle guardrail calls, confirmed live, ids `11044`/`11065`, both `patterns_used: ["guardrails","prompt-chaining"]`). Independently re-verified: 2 real `guardrails-check` rows from the fresh live test both resolved correctly against the new catalog entry. | ✅ Done | S-LOG-12-13-14-design/S-LOG-12-13-14 (v6.3.17, `d3883a5`) |
| LOG-16 | **Found + fixed 2026-07-16, John's platform-rules objection: a pattern must never require a Supabase-declared field to be logged.** Verified live: `sendRequest()` (`request-receivable.js`) spread a declared Intent Skill Profile field (`intent_technical_services`) into `patterns_used` unconditionally on every call of that intent, regardless of whether that specific call actually exercised the pattern. Quantified: 659 real rows carried this risk (excludes `lib/librarian.js`'s hardcoded-at-real-occurrence `rag`/`embeddings`, confirmed honest regardless of age via direct code read). Established a 4-bucket design principle with John (mechanical / declared-triggers-behavior / self-reported / deterministically-inferred) governing all future pattern-logging work. Fixed: `buildPatternsUsed()` now reads `ai-enrichment.js`'s already-computed real per-call `debug.rag_retrieved`/`reflect_ran`/`synthesis_ran` booleans instead of the declared list — almost entirely wiring, since the real detection already existed. Added a historical trust boundary (`PATTERN_VERIFICATION_CUTOFF`, `isPatternTrusted()`) to `computeByPattern()` so pre-fix rows' unverifiable patterns stop displaying — per John's explicit rule, if it can't be verified, don't show it; if it can, show it. Node test 9/9 PASS, build clean, `git diff --stat` exactly 2 files. **Independently re-verified by the design session against real Supabase data, not accepted from the coding report alone:** pulled the two cited rows directly — id `11248` (18:42:04 UTC, before the `18:44:00Z` cutoff) and id `11252` (18:46:18 UTC, after) — and hand-applied `isPatternTrusted()`'s logic, confirming they bracket the cutoff exactly as designed. `LOG-15` (drawer capability-label mislabeling, found same session) and `LOG-17` (Phase 2 — `llm-as-judge`/Case-Based-Reasoning detection, `streaming` detection) logged as separate open backlog items, not fixed this session. | ✅ Done | S-LOG-16-design/S-LOG-16 (v6.3.20, `5e6a9ea`) |
| LOG-21 | **Found + fixed 2026-07-16, John's direct ask: do the AI Audit screen and the MI screen's Agents/Agent Routing panel reconcile? (`S-AUDITLOG-design`/`S-LOG-21`).** They were two independently-implemented read paths over the same `ai_activity_log` table (`useAIActivity()`'s `byAgent` vs. `useAgentActivitySummary()`/`buildActivitySummary()`) sharing only low-level cost/dedup helpers, not the aggregation code itself. Live-checked pre-fix: 8,737 vs. 8,410 calls, ~$38 vs. ~$37 est. cost — close only because current traffic is almost entirely MI-loop activity within 30 days, not by structural guarantee. **John's explicit call: build a shared aggregation core, not just document the divergence.** Fixed: `classifyRow()`/`buildActivitySummary()`/`percentile()` moved verbatim from `useAgents.js` into `useAIActivity.js` (the file both hooks already treat as canonical, per `AI-51`); `useAIActivity()`'s `byAgent` now calls the same shared function via a row-shape adapter instead of its own hand-written loop. Output shape unchanged (`AIActivityPanel.jsx` untouched). This does **not** make the two screens' totals reconcile — `MI_LOOP_SCOPE`/`RECENCY_WINDOW_DAYS` stay intentionally different; it guarantees the underlying math can never silently diverge between the two call sites again. Survived a mid-session rebase onto a concurrent session's (`AI-53`) substantial rewrite of the same file's top section — confirmed by direct code read that both coexist correctly. Node test 6/6 PASS, build clean, `git diff --stat` exactly 2 files. **Independently re-verified live against the real dev preview:** AI Audit's `BY AGENT` section (the exact rewired path) rendering all 20 agent rows correctly, zero console errors; MI screen's own Agents panel also confirmed unaffected. | ✅ Done | S-AUDITLOG-design/S-LOG-21 (v6.3.37, `01184f6`) |
| LOG-20 | **Found + fixed 2026-07-16, John's cross-screen AI Audit consistency check (`S-AUDITLOG-design`/`S-LOG-20`).** `src/aiPatterns.js`'s `AI_PAT`/`AGENT_PATTERNS` constants (used by 8 screens' step/task badges) were a hand-maintained duplicate of pattern display strings, entirely independent of `shared/ai-patterns.js`'s canonical `PATTERN_CATALOG` (the same catalog `useAIActivity.js`/`lib/activity-log.js` already treat as ground truth per `AA-190b`). No live drift found, but nothing structurally connected the two. Fixed: added a `names(...slugs)` lookup helper sourcing every `AI_PAT` string from `PATTERN_CATALOG`'s own `name` field — every string confirmed byte-identical to its pre-fix value (19/19 Node test checks PASS), zero visible change. Live spot-check on the Roster screen's `KNOWLEDGE_TRAINING` badge tooltip confirmed. `git diff --stat` exactly 1 file. | ✅ Done | S-AUDITLOG-design/S-LOG-20 (v6.3.36, `fa20694`) |
| LOG-19 | **Found + fixed 2026-07-16, John's cross-screen AI Audit consistency check (`S-AUDITLOG-design`/`S-LOG-19`).** `useAgents.js`'s comment above `useAgentActivitySummary()` still described `useAIActivity()`'s `byAgent` as "backed by an in-memory log capped at 500 rows (`hydrateFromSupabase()`'s `.limit(500)`)" — stale since `AI-184` replaced that cap with full `.range()` pagination. Cosmetic only (no code read the old cap), but exactly the kind of locked-comment drift that misleads a future session reading the file cold. Fixed: comment corrected to describe the real current distinction between the two hooks (fetch trigger/lifecycle, not row coverage). `git diff --stat` confirmed exactly 1 file. | ✅ Done | S-AUDITLOG-design/S-LOG-19 (v6.3.35, `ee0bc18`) |
| LOG-18 | **Found live 2026-07-16 during `S-AI-46b`'s own Category L test, root-caused and fixed same day (`S-LOG-18-design`/`S-LOG-18-activity-delivery`).** `lib/activity-log.js`'s shared `logActivity()` is deliberately fire-and-forget (bare un-awaited `fetch()`) — confirmed via 3 independent live observations (100% reproduction, not intermittent) that any call fired as the *last* async work before a handler responds (REFLECT/synthesis, `ai-enrichment.js`'s final steps) gets silently abandoned when Vercel tears down the function's execution context right after the response is sent, while calls fired earlier in a request (with substantial subsequent awaited work still ahead) always survive. Platform-wide risk, not `reflect`/`synthesis`-specific. Fixed: wrapped the write in Vercel's `waitUntil()` (`@vercel/functions`, added as a new dependency) inside `logActivity()` itself — one file, zero call-site changes, same shared-service-once principle `AI-46` used. One genuine unknown flagged before shipping (this codebase's classic `(req, res)` handler shape vs. `waitUntil()`'s documented `fetch(request)`-shape examples, an untested combination) resolved live: works correctly. **Independently re-verified by the design session against real Supabase data, not accepted from the coding report alone:** the decisive test's own `reflect`/`synthesis` rows (ids `11647`/`11646`, `agent_id: dan`, `feature: ai-enrichment`) confirmed to exist exactly as reported; 2 dedicated regression calls (a simple `channel-intelligence` call and a full multi-hop `quality-gate`→`channel-intelligence`→`project-manager` delegation chain, 16 rows total) confirmed no breakage. No Task 1b fallback needed. Node test 2/2, build clean, `git diff --stat` exactly 3 files (`lib/activity-log.js`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json`). | ✅ Done | S-LOG-18-design/S-LOG-18-activity-delivery (v6.3.31, `c5857c6`) |

| AG-33 | Eleanor Voss (LB-01, the Librarian) — her first real Capability, `data-room-custody`, closing the `AR-1.3` gap. **Redesigned mid-session (John, design review, 2026-07-02) after the first live run found a real platform gap**: the original design had callers hand her a pre-filled object (`operation`/`data_room_tag`/`title`/`content`) — but `task_context` object fields never reach the model's visible prompt unless shaped as a `goal` string (only the "Work Order" pattern in `db-assembly.js` renders them), so her first live call received nothing describing the task and invented a `data_room_tag`, correctly failing loudly (FK violation) instead of corrupting data. Redesigned around her actual stated purpose instead of patching around the failure: she is the platform's best query/retrieval specialist — callers now hand her a plain-language description, she searches `the_Library` herself (new `eleanor-knowledge` Skill Profile, `traits.source: "the_library"`, reusing the existing RAG mechanism unchanged), then decides the operation/parameters. Real reasoning, not a rubber stamp. Executes exactly one `writeLibrary()` operation per call (`insert`/`update_status` only — `bulk_reset`/Demo Reset deliberately excluded from her schema, stays human-triggered, `S-APPLE-04c`), via a new generic `library-write` handler (`api/_lib/handlers/library-write.js`) dispatched through `request-receivable.js`'s existing `format_contract.handler` registry — same pattern `store.js` already established, not a second write path. **A real tenant-isolation gap found and fixed** (`lib/librarian.js`): `the_library.supersedes_id`'s foreign key only confirms the referenced row exists, not that it's in the *same* `data_room_tag` — a cross-Data-Room supersede would have silently succeeded; now refused (`denied-supersedes-cross-room`). **A real wiring gap found and fixed** (`db-assembly.js`/`ai-enrichment.js`): `queryLibrary()` already accepted a `data_room_tag` param but nothing threaded one through for `the_library`-sourced RAG — needed for any `uber_access` holder (only Eleanor today) to search at all, since her credential branch requires an explicit tag. Both fixes are generic (read new optional traits, gate on nothing agent-specific) — benefit any future caller, not Eleanor-specific conditionals. **Considered and explicitly rejected**: a separate "Bouncer" access-control agent (`AR-3.1` already assigns full CRUD gatekeeping to Eleanor alone, no second persona needed) and checking the *original* delegating agent's own credentials (needs a generic harness change nothing does today, zero practical effect with only one Data Room existing). First of a 3-session chain (`S-LIBRARIAN-04` → `S-ARCH-HITL-RESUME-01` → `S-APPLE-04b`). Node test 28/28 PASS across categories F/L/L-tenant-isolation/M, including real live insert + real live archive via plain-language input (no pre-filled fields) and a deterministic direct-`writeLibrary()` test for the tenant-isolation fix (kept separate from Eleanor's own LLM reasoning, which is inherently non-deterministic). One additional gap found and fixed by the coding agent, reviewed and approved independently by Claude (not just accepted from the report): `request-receivable.js`'s Step 5 return only ever forwarded `deliverable_id`, silently dropping every other field a handler returns — fixed with an additive `...result` spread, verified byte-identical for `store.js` (which only ever returns `{deliverable_id}`). Manual QA: all 12 items independently verified directly by Claude — Supabase rows (4 Skill Profiles, correct `display_order`), git log/status/diff review of all 6 files, zero orphaned test fixtures, serverless count unchanged at 11. Vercel logs accepted on indirect evidence (no deploy in this environment). Client-side (`MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`): `readSSEResult()` mirrors `CreateWorkOrderScreen.jsx`'s existing reader; live delegation events and the pre-existing scripted macro-hop swaps both feed one shared `setStatus()`/`workingStatus`, two-tone styled (`kind: 'scripted'` vs `'orchestration'`) so a viewer can tell "this always happens" from "an agent just decided this." Final receipt: `"Full Agent Routing & Answer Given in Xs"` caption on the answer bubble (`msg.totalElapsedMs`, a simple end-start diff). Also fixed 2 pre-existing `SERVICE_CATALOG` gaps found along the way (`channel-intelligence` missing `Agent Delegation` despite `AA-164` already using it) and broadened the `streaming` `PATTERN_CATALOG` description to honestly cover event-granularity, not just token-granularity. **Forward-compatible with `AA-171`:** the guardrail-block path's hardcoded `agent_id:"sam"` call bypasses the harness loop entirely (screen-scripted), so it's structurally invisible to this mechanism today — correctly gets only the plain scripted styling until `AA-171` routes it through real agent reasoning, at which point it lands on an already-instrumented dispatch point automatically, zero new code. | MI-44 | **Work-dropdown click-interception hotfix.** John's direct live bug report right after `S-MI-32`/`MI-36` shipped: dropdown items didn't respond to clicks, first click on "Work" appeared to do nothing. Root-caused via `elementFromPoint` hit-testing, not guessed: the click-outside backdrop (`zIndex:998`) is a sibling of the nav wrapper, but that wrapper has `transform:translateX(-50%)`, which creates its own CSS stacking context — the dropdown's own `zIndex:999` is only compared *inside* that context, so from the backdrop's side the whole wrapper had no z-index at all, and the backdrop (998) painted and captured clicks above the entire Work tab and dropdown. Fixed with one added `zIndex:1000` on the nav wrapper. Live-verified the full repro sequence (fresh load → click Work → click Bench → click Work again → click a dashboard item) end to end, matching exactly what was reported. Also added the requested small gold (`T.brassLight`) down-arrow dropdown hint (▼, 7px, non-interactive). Node test 3/3 PASS, build clean. Renumbered `MI-42`→`43`→`44` (`v6.1.43`→`44`→`45`) during rebase — 2 other concurrent same-day sessions claimed those IDs/versions first, resolved cleanly (no file-region overlap). No formal kickoff doc — direct root-cause investigation and fix by the design session itself, given the live-bug-report context. | ✅ Done | S-MI-44 (v6.1.45, hotfix) |

| MI-58 | **Fix "expect > Xs" estimate depth-blindness.** John's direct live-reported bug (screenshot: "expect > 30s" badge sitting far below real elapsed time, 53s and counting). Root-caused live against real `ai_activity_log` data, not guessed: `estimateChainMs()` summed one blended `avgLatency` per intent across the `qa` chain (routing→answer→review→display), treating each as exactly one LLM turn — but `execute.js`'s `:depthN` tag showed `ci-answer-intent`/`qg-review-intent` commonly loop through 2-3 real turns before a single frontend call returns (e.g. `ci-answer-intent` reached depth1 on ~69% of calls), so the blended single-hop average structurally undercounted the real per-call cost. Confirmed with John before coding (two explicit judgment calls, not assumed): use **p75 per depth** rather than the mean (a more conservative "expect >" floor), and add a **30-day recency window** (confirmed live this is a no-op today — all 4 qa-chain intents' data only goes back to 2026-07-07, matters going forward as history accumulates). **Fix, 2 files, additive only:** `useAgents.js`'s `buildActivitySummary()` gains a per-depth `byDepth` breakdown (p75 + calls per depth) alongside the existing `avgLatency`/`maxLatency`/`calls`/`byModel` fields, which stay byte-identical — the Agents drawer needed zero code changes. `estimateChainMs()` rewritten to sum each hop's depth0 p75 plus every further depth's p75 weighted by how often that depth is actually reached (expected-value-per-call instead of expected-value-per-turn); `formatExpectation()`/all 3 call sites unchanged. Node test 8/8 PASS, build clean, `git status` confirmed exactly the 2 intended files touched. **Design session's own live verification against the real deployed dev preview, not accepted from the coding report alone:** ran the identical EMEA co-op-budget question from John's original screenshot — initial "expect < 2m" ceiling still showed immediately on submit; once routing resolved, the badge upgraded to "expect > 1m 5s" (vs. the old ~25-30s), and the real run (which happened to hit a self-flag→guardrail-block escalation path deeper than the standard 4-hop chain) completed in 75.0s, confirming the new estimate is now in the right neighborhood instead of off by 2x+. Also confirmed a real production hop the fix's diagnosis predicted: Owen's quality gate genuinely routed back to Marcus mid-run for a retry, exactly the loop the old estimate never accounted for. Agents drawer spot-checked after a **fresh page reload at 1280×800** (not a live resize — same `matchMedia`-on-mount tooling quirk already documented by `MI-50`/`MI-51`, confirmed again here) — all `byKind` avg/max/calls rows rendered identically to their pre-fix shape and values, no regression from the additive `byDepth` field. killing the one hop already running server-side at the instant of Clear is a separate harness-level item, logged as `HAR-03`, deliberately not folded in; (3) a line + timestamp divider renders above the first hop-card of each new question, snapshotted at the moment `submit()` begins. **Known accepted approximation**, same precedent as `CHI-03c`'s hop-range badges: if the last hop before a boundary and the first hop after it share the same `agentId`, `groupEventsIntoTurns` already merges them into one card, so the divider lands slightly imprecisely on the merged card — not worth solving beyond that. **Resolved during design (2026-07-16):** implemented as a synthetic `question_boundary` marker event (not an index-based approximation) — a marker always starts its own turn-grouping entry regardless of neighboring `agentId`, so the merge-approximation risk described above does not actually occur; Root-caused via a live timed reproduction against the deployed dev URL (33.4s total, HTTP 200 that particular run): `execute.js`'s delegation loop calls `callModel()` once per turn for the *delegating* agent's own reasoning (deciding to `request_help`, later deciding who to `delegate_to_agent`) — those calls wrote zero row to `ai_activity_log`, not even `null`, the literal blind spot `AA-118`'s own diagnostic method (querying that table for gaps) couldn't see. (2) `request-receivable.js`'s `REQUEST_HELP_TOOL`/`DELEGATE_TO_AGENT_TOOL` both gain `maxLength: 300` on their `reasoning` property, platform-wide (every capability using this pattern benefits) — an instructional signal to the model, not a server-enforced hard cap, framed honestly as such. (3) Supabase: `ci-answer-display-intent`'s `max_tokens` 4000→800 + a brevity sentence appended to `method`; `schema`/`can_request_help`/`llm_model` unchanged. **Same honesty as `AA-118`: a trim + instrumentation fix, not a structural elimination of the single-request-budget risk** — `MAX_LOOP_DEPTH`, Michelle's non-unilateral-selection rule, and Owen's retry-confirmation step were explicitly left untouched (locked `ARCHITECTURE.md` §19d/§19e decisions). | AA-113 | **`callModel()` retries once on a transient non-2xx Anthropic HTTP error, instead of failing immediately with zero trace.** Originally found 2026-07-04 (`S-MARKET-INTEL-01d-design`'s own QA: an Accept on a `pending_confirmation` 500'd once, succeeded cleanly on an immediate manual retry, exactly one `the_reasoning` row written, no duplicate). Never scheduled until re-surfaced 2026-07-07 while investigating a second live "Something went wrong reaching Marcus" report, distinct from `AA-120` (already shipped): John confirmed the symptom occurs even when Owen's quality-gate retry path never fires, ruling out a chain-specific latency explanation. Checked directly rather than assumed: the failed `ci-answer-display-intent` call's own `ai_activity_log` trail showed only ~21s of logged activity before going silent (nowhere near the 60s ceiling), and `MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`'s `callCapability()` has no client-side timeout of its own — ruling out both a latency-ceiling cause and a client-side-abort cause. This left `callModel()`'s complete absence of retry on a transient Anthropic error (shared by every agent's every turn, at every depth of the delegation loop) as the best fit: a single bad API response anywhere in the chain throws immediately, with zero trace, since `AA-120`'s `logAgentTurn()` only logs a turn that already succeeded. **Fix, 2 files:** `request-receivable.js` gains `postToAnthropicWithRetry()` — retries once, with a 1s backoff, on `429`/`500`/`502`/`503`/`529` only; any other status (a malformed request, an auth failure) still fails fast, unchanged, since retrying those would never help. Wraps only the initial call in `callModel()` — the existing, separate parse-failure retry (a schema-conformance issue, not an HTTP error) is deliberately left untouched. `execute.js`'s `logAgentTurn()` (from `AA-120`) gains an `api_retry_count` param, surfaced as a `:apiRetryN` suffix on the logged `feature` string, so a retry is directly visible in `ai_activity_log` without needing raw Vercel logs. Node test 14/14 PASS (Category B source checks; Category G mocked transient-529-then-success and non-transient-400-fails-fast cases, deterministic rather than waiting for a real API hiccup; Category L live call), build clean. A genuine, separate latency risk, left open pending a structural-fix-vs-trim decision with John. | ✅ Done | S-ARCH-API-RETRY-01-design/S-ARCH-API-RETRY-01 (v6.0.30, bfce649) |
| AA-108 | **Silent-fabrication bug, fixed.** Michelle's `agent-selection-intent` was hallucinating `intent_slug` values — its schema required one per candidate, but `lib/project-manager.js`'s roster broker only ever supplied `capability_slug`, giving the model no ground truth. Found live during `S-ARCH-HITL-RESUME-02`'s Category L test (2026-07-03, the first live test to ever exercise `request_help`→`delegate_to_agent` through to a capability needing a specific intent): the model guessed a plausible but nonexistent slug (`library-write-execution` instead of the real `library-write-intent`), and `db-assembly.js`'s `assemblePrompt()` silently filtered to zero Intent Skill Profiles instead of erroring — so the downstream call proceeded with no schema/handler, and Eleanor's model turn free-wrote a fully fabricated, realistic "✅ INSERTED" confirmation. Independently confirmed by direct query: zero `the_library` rows existed anywhere in the entire test window, despite every layer reporting success. **Fix, two parts:** (1) `lib/project-manager.js`'s `fetchRoster()`/`rosterToContext()` now join `capability_skill_profiles`+`skill_profiles` to surface each capability's real intent slugs in the roster context, generically, for every agent — same mechanism `capability_slug` already used, no hardcoded values anywhere; `agent-selection-intent` got explicit instructions to copy exactly or return `null`, never guess. (2) `assemblePrompt()` now throws a clear, capability-and-slug-naming error when a caller passes an `intent_slug` that doesn't match any loaded Intent Skill Profile, instead of silently degrading — the platform's "fail safe, never fake" principle applied structurally, same class as `resolveCapabilityHolder()`'s existing unhandled throws. **Conclusive live proof:** re-ran `S-ARCH-HITL-RESUME-02`'s exact Escalate propose→accept scenario end to end — this time it landed a real, uniquely-marked `the_library` row (not a loose filter match), independently re-verified directly against Supabase by the design session using a fresh script with a different deliberately-wrong `intent_slug` than the original bug, confirming the guard is generic, not special-cased. | MI-18 | **Agent Activity drawer, Column 3 — merges the two previously-overlapping, never-reconciled `MI-06`/`MI-18` backlog entries into one drawer, resolving the content-duplication `MI-17` had flagged but deliberately left for a later session.** John asked to design "the 3rd column, agent list drawer." Each card now shows both identity (avatar/name/role/specialty chips) and live usage (calls/avg latency/avg cost) together — what `MI-06` (AI Audit framing) and `MI-18` (roster framing) each wanted separately. **Design revision, confirmed with John:** rejected the original `MI-06` plan of gating the drawer on `AI-38` (generalizing the unused `isAppleChannel` boolean into a `section` field on `agents.js`). Instead built a portable, page-agnostic mechanism: any screen supplies its own hardcoded `proposedAgentIds` list (lives in the page's own file, not on the agent record), and Active-vs-Potential status is derived live from whether `ai_activity_log` has any row for that `agent_id` — never a stored flag, so it self-populates the moment an agent's first real call lands and never reverts. `AI-38`'s remaining scope narrowed at this session's kickoff to the global AI Audit screen's By Agent grouping only (untouched, unscheduled). **Real constraint found and designed around, not discovered live:** `useAIActivity()`'s `byAgent` aggregation is backed by an in-memory log capped at 500 rows (`hydrateFromSupabase()`'s `.limit(500)`) — a rolling window, not full history, so an occasionally-used agent could scroll out of it and be wrongly demoted back to "Potential." The new `useAgentActivitySummary(agentIds)` hook (`useAgents.js`) queries Supabase directly and independently of that cap. Deliberately did **not** reuse or modify `useAgentUsageCounts()` (`RO-09`): that hook's flat `{agentId: number}` shape is a direct sort-comparator input in `RosterScreen.jsx` (`usageCounts[a.id] || 0`) — changing its shape would have silently broken that sort. **Fix, 3 files, 4 tasks:** `useAgentActivitySummary()` (`useAgents.js`); the "Agents" `Drawer` wired into `AuditColumn` (`MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`) reusing the generic `Drawer` component `MI-17` already built (zero changes to it), rendered below the untouched "Learned Context" drawer, with `opacity: 0.38` for muted Potential cards (direct reuse of `STYLE-GUIDE.md`'s existing "Unavailable agents" rule from Agent Picker Cards, not a new value); dead `isAppleChannel` field removed from all 6 agent rows in `agents.js` (confirmed zero remaining reads anywhere in `src/` before removal, its only purpose fully superseded by the page-local `proposedAgentIds` list). Zero new `api/` files (serverless count unchanged, 11 of 12). **Real bug found and root-caused in the coding session, not shipped as-is:** the kickoff's own `useAgentActivitySummary()` code used `d.calls` as the null-check for `avgCost` instead of a cost-specific counter — mirroring the bug `avgLatency` already correctly avoided via `latencyCount`. An agent with real calls but zero cost data (e.g. Priya, confirmed live) would have rendered a fabricated `$0.00` instead of `—`. Fixed with a parallel `costCount`, applied in both the shipped hook and its Node test. Node test 17/17 PASS (Category F schema alignment, Category B aggregation/bucket-split logic, Category M dead-field-removal cross-reference check via a real `fs`-based grep of `src/`). Build clean (910 modules, 9.59s, zero errors, unchanged module count). **Independently re-verified by the design session, not accepted from the coding report alone:** `git diff` confirmed the exact 3-file change matches the kickoff plus the approved `costCount` fix; re-ran an independent Node re-verification script (not the coding session's own copy) directly against the shipped files, 19/19 substantive checks PASS; confirmed `SharedUI.jsx`/`RosterScreen.jsx`/`useAIActivity.js`/`AIActivityPanel.jsx` all byte-identical (`git diff` empty) — no scope creep into any protected file. **Live browser verification, worked around the same `launch.json`-resolves-against-main-checkout limitation `MI-17` first hit:** ran an isolated `npx vite --port 5180` directly against this worktree (its own `.env.local` copied in locally, gitignored, never committed) plus `claude-in-chrome` — confirmed the drawer renders "6 active · 0 potential" exactly matching a direct Supabase cross-check (`marcus`/`priya`/`nadia`/`owen`/`sam`/`elena`, 201/109/66/61/20/14 real calls each); confirmed every active card's Calls/Avg Latency/Avg Cost against the raw data, including one non-obvious match worth noting: Elena Cho displayed `0.6s` avg latency against a naive `avg(latency_ms)` SQL check that predicted `0.4s` — investigated rather than dismissed, and confirmed correct: 5 of her 14 rows have `latency_ms: 0` (a pre-existing quirk in that particular write path, not introduced by this session), and the shipped `if (row.latency_ms)` guard correctly excludes them from the average — the exact same falsy-latency-exclusion pattern `useAIActivity.js`'s existing `byAgent` aggregation already uses, not a bug. Confirmed `$0.00` (Marcus, real near-zero cost) vs. `—` (all 5 others, genuinely null cost data, not fabricated) render correctly. Confirmed zero console errors on load and after toggling both drawers, confirmed "Learned Context" and Pipeline Log fully unchanged/independent. **One item not live-exercised, honestly noted:** all 6 proposed agents are currently Active in production, so the muted-Potential card styling itself wasn't visually confirmed live this session — proven correct by code review and the Node test's bucket-split logic only. Ran in its own git worktree (`session/agent-activity-drawer`) per the concurrent-sessions protocol, zero collisions. | AA-131 | **Capped `qg-review-intent`'s own uncapped `eval.critique`/`guardrail.reason` schema fields — same uncapped-free-text-field class as `AA-113`/`AA-120`/`AA-124`/`AA-128`, broader reach since these two fields generate on every single Q&A review (pass or fail), not just a rare hand-off path.** Found 2026-07-07 evaluating whether fixing Owen's latency would help platform speed: real `deliverables` content showed `eval.critique` running 500-650 characters on every call and `guardrail.reason` running ~950 characters on a real live block, enumerated (1)(2)(3) reasoning — never checked before since no real block existed to measure against until `AA-130`'s live incident. **Two different caps, two different reasons:** `eval.critique` already passes through `shapeForLog()` (`AA-125`) before reaching the Pipeline Log — the model's full text is never shown to the user, so capping it is a pure generation-time win with zero display tradeoff; `guardrail.reason` is concatenated raw, unshaped, directly into the MI screen's failure banner (`buildFailureText()`, `AA-130`) — capping it is both a speed win and a UX improvement, not a tradeoff. **Confirmed separate from Owen's ~50s regenerate-chain** (Marcus's real regeneration cost, not field verbosity — not touched by this session). **Fix, 1 Supabase row, 0 code files:** `jsonb_set` added `maxLength: 400` to `eval.critique`, `maxLength: 300` to `guardrail.reason`. `schema.required`, `eval.scores`'s per-dimension `minimum`/`maximum`, `guardrail.result`/`rule_violated` enums, `final_answer` shape, and `method` all confirmed byte-identical before/after via direct Supabase re-query; `agent-selection-intent`'s row (`AA-128`) independently re-queried and confirmed untouched. Also checked whether "type" should be the platform's existing `PATTERN_CATALOG` (RAG/ReAct/Tool Use/Agent Delegation/etc., `useAIActivity.js`) — confirmed that taxonomy is real and already used by the global AI Audit's By Pattern view, but `logAgentTurn()` (`execute.js`) writes `patterns_used: []` on every non-delegating turn, including all of Marcus's slowest rows — deferred as its own future item (queue), shipped this cheaper turn-kind breakdown instead per John's explicit call. **Design correction made mid-investigation, not silently:** the original approved plan ("exclude all `sendRequest()` wrapper rows — they duplicate the terminal turn's own latency") was live-checked before building it and found wrong: only a minority of wrapper rows actually pair with a nearby `agent-turn` (`data-analysis` 0/21, `memory-consolidation` 0/13, `hypothesis-evaluation` 5/73, `channel-intelligence` 28/145, `project-manager` 28/133, `screen-controls` 21/49) — a blanket per-`ai_type` exclusion would have zeroed out Nadia's and Elena's entire by-kind data (they have zero `agent-turn` rows at all). **Fix, 2 files, 3 tasks:** `useAgentActivitySummary()` (`useAgents.js`) gains a per-row, 2-second timestamp-pairing check (`classifyRow()`) instead of a blanket rule — only genuine duplicate pairs are excluded, everything else (including every wrapper-only agent's full activity) is kept and correctly attributed to its own kind; top-level `avgLatency`/`totalLatency`/`latencyCount` removed entirely (not just hidden — nothing else read it); `calls`/`avgCost` unchanged. Card UI (`MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`) drops the blended `StatCell`, adds a sorted-descending by-kind list with a generic `formatKindLabel()` title-case formatter — deliberately not a hardcoded per-intent label map (would need manual upkeep per new intent across 10 agents), a disclosed, deliberate deviation from the approved mock's hand-written wording ("Answer generation" vs. shipped "Ci Answer Intent"). **Real bug found and fixed in the coding session, not shipped as designed:** the kickoff's own Node test mock data had an off-by-one (asserted `marcus.calls === 4` against a fixture containing only 3 Marcus rows) — root-caused to the test fixture, not the aggregation logic, which was correct throughout; fixed to `=== 3`. | AA-144 | **Fixed the nested `final_delegation` shape bug — a recursive `delegate_to_agent(is_final:true)` hand-off misfired `AA-126`'s empty-content guard**, found live 2026-07-07 during `S-ARCH-DELEGATION-REQUIRED-01`'s (`AA-142`) own post-fix verification on the "fail" MI seed question (Vietnam reseller network). Root cause: the `final_delegation` shape (`execute.js`, the `is_final` branch) spread the delegate's real content flat at the top level with no `.content` key at all, while the guard checked `delegateResult.content` — correct for the ordinary `sendRequest()` shape, but reads `undefined` when `delegateResult` is itself a nested `final_delegation` object, throwing "returned no content" even though real content existed. **Fix, 1 file, 3 tasks:** extracted `buildFinalDelegationResult()` (new named export in `execute.js`) from the inline `is_final` block — the one behavioral change is that it now always mirrors real content under a `.content` key alongside the existing flat spread, making the shape self-describing at any recursion depth; the empty-content guard itself is unchanged (still throws on genuine no-content). Node.js unit test (4 cases, including a direct nested-`final_delegation`-as-`delegateResult` regression case) confirms the fix logically; Category L live test against the 3 real seed questions directly observed the fix firing on a genuine `final_delegation` result. **Independently re-verified by the design session against the real deployed dev URL, not accepted from the coding report alone:** all 3 seed questions confirmed working live via `claude-in-chrome` — Vietnam (rendered via `AA-137`'s existing decline/`NEEDS REVIEW` fallback, not a crash), EMEA (hit `AA-141`'s already-known transient `continue` 500 on the first attempt, self-resolved cleanly on retry with a real 2-hop Marcus→Michelle→Alex delegation, correct `confidence_tier`), Japan (routed to the Theory flow, correct intent classification). Zero unexpected console/network errors across all runs. Honest caveat carried from the coding session: a true two-level-deep nested `is_final` chain was not observed live (not reliably forceable on demand) — the Node.js test is the deterministic proof of the fix itself. New finding from the coding session's own live test spun out to its own row (`AA-145`, not left buried here): intermittent genuinely-empty-content case on `alex`/`screen-controls`/`qa-answer-format`, correctly caught by the unweakened guard. Ran in the `mi-loop-reliability` worktree, zero collisions. (2) content gap — Nadia's 4 intent Skill Profiles all had `objective`/`output_desc` null, giving Michelle zero real signal versus Sam Reyes's `intake-failure-intent`, which already has a precise, matching objective for exactly this "guardrail failure → decide escalate?" task shape. **Confirmed with John these are complementary, not duplicate fixes:** the code fix is capability-agnostic (names no specific agent) and stays necessary permanently since `intent_slug: null` is a deliberate, ongoing design possibility for any capability; the content fix only reduces how often Nadia specifically gets misrouted to. **✅ Done, live-verified 2026-07-14:** shipped 1 file (`db-assembly.js`, the `else` fallback), plus the 4 Supabase content updates. Node test could not run in the coding session's own worktree (no `.env.local`, expected). **Design session's own live verification against the real deployed dev endpoint (not synthetic):** fired a real call to `data-analysis` with `intent_slug: null` — response was a coherent, sensible clarifying-question reply (no garbled instruction stacking), correctly empty `patterns_used` (an honest "nothing intent-specific happened" result, not a mis-attributed one). Re-queried all 4 Supabase rows, confirmed `objective`/`output_desc` match exactly what was written. Test confirmation cleaned up (rejected) after verification, no live Data Room write occurred. | LOO-001 | **[Continuity]** Michelle's roster broker returned every active agent unfiltered, including the requesting agent itself — when the only genuine capability match was the requester's own, her reasoning could recommend the requester back to itself, a structurally meaningless hop that burns a hop off the budget for zero new information. **Fixed as reasoning, not filtering** (`S-ARCH-LOOP-CONTINUITY-01`, v6.3.28): `execute.js`'s `request_help` dispatch threads a new `requesting_agent_id` field into Michelle's `task_context`; her `agent-selection-intent` objective/schema (bundled with `LOO-002`'s no-match output) teaches her to treat a self-only match as no match at all. **Patch found live 2026-07-16 (`S-ARCH-LOOP-CONTINUITY-01-patch`, v6.3.30):** the v6.3.28 fix's own live verification found only 1/6 self-only-match trials actually returned `no_match: true` — the model's own `reasoning` field explicitly named the self-referral conflict, then overrode it anyway. Root-caused live: `agent-selection-intent.temperature` was `null`, never brought under the existing `AA-154` precedent (`ci-answer-intent`/`qg-review-intent` both already pinned to `temperature: 0`) for this exact reasoning-text-vs-output-field inconsistency failure class. Pinned `temperature=0` (confirmed `null` before, `0` after via direct query); expanded live re-test (8 trials against the real dev endpoint, up from the original 6) came back 8/8 clean (`no_match: true`, `recommended_agent_id: null`, Marcus never recommended to himself), both regression scenarios (nadia/tax-filing generic no-match, marcus→owen legitimate `quality-gate` match) unaffected. | ✅ Done | S-ARCH-LOOP-CONTINUITY-01-design/S-ARCH-LOOP-CONTINUITY-01 (v6.3.28) + -patch-design/-patch (v6.3.30) |
| LOO-002 | **[Feature]** Michelle's `agent-selection-intent` schema had no legitimate "no good fit" output — `recommended_agent_id`/`recommended_capability_slug` were required non-null strings, forcing a confident-sounding pick even when nobody genuinely fit (a real contributor to `AA-194`'s live Sam Reyes misroute). **Fixed** (`S-ARCH-LOOP-CONTINUITY-01`, v6.3.28): added `no_match`/`missing_skillset` to the schema, both recommendation fields made nullable, bundled with `LOO-001`'s self-referral rule. **Re-verified 2026-07-16** during the `v6.3.30` patch's expanded live re-test: the generic no-match scenario (nadia, tax-filing) correctly populated `missing_skillset` and nulled both recommendation fields on all runs, unaffected by the patch's `temperature` change. | ✅ Done | S-ARCH-LOOP-CONTINUITY-01-design/S-ARCH-LOOP-CONTINUITY-01 (v6.3.28) |
| LOO-004 | **[Architecture]** `execute.js`'s `resolveCapabilityHolder()` (resolves the single holder of `project-manager` for every `request_help` call, and of any `critique_capability_slug` for a consequential-action gate) queried `agent_capability_assignments` with no `is_active` filter, unlike the roster broker's own separate gate — currently latent (zero inactive agents platform-wide, confirmed live) but would silently resolve to a defunct holder the day one is deactivated. **Fixed** (`S-ARCH-LOOP-CONTINUITY-01`, v6.3.28): added a second query mirroring `isActiveAgent()`'s existing idiom; a deactivated holder now throws the identical "no agent currently holds capability" error already thrown for zero rows — no new error shape, no new decision logic. Confirmed sniff-test-legitimate (`ARCHITECTURE.md` §19d) — this primitive resolves a single named role, never selects among candidates. Live-verified `resolveCapabilityHolder('project-manager')` still resolves to `michelle` (genuinely active) on the happy path; the inactive-holder branch itself proven via mocked Node.js test only, deliberately not live-toggled against real production agents with 5-7 concurrent sessions active. | ✅ Done | S-ARCH-LOOP-CONTINUITY-01-design/S-ARCH-LOOP-CONTINUITY-01 (v6.3.28) |

| AA-138 | Live production failure, confirmed against real `ai_activity_log` timestamps: `runCapability()` (`execute.js`) recursed in-process for every `request_help`/`delegate_to_agent` hop, all sharing one Lambda invocation's `maxDuration: 60`; `MAX_LOOP_DEPTH` capped hop *count*, not *time*. | AA-139 | Retrofit of `AA-138`'s POC into the real `runCapability()` loop, John's go-ahead 2026-07-07. Hybrid design: `runCapability()` stays fully in-process/synchronous by default (zero added latency for the common case); a shared `runLoop()` helper checks remaining wall-clock against the shared `maxDuration: 60` budget before each hop — only checkpoints to `durable_hops` and returns `{status:'in_progress', job_id}` when a hop risks the ceiling. `_deadline` threaded through nested calls exactly like the existing `_hop_counter` pattern, so the shared ceiling stays real across delegation hops. POC's duplicate loop (`lib/durable-loop-poc.js`) retired per `ARCHITECTURE.md` §19b. Split into 2 sub-sessions (exceeded 3-file/4-task budget, 2 layers): `S-ARCH-DURABLE-LOOP-02a` — harness (`runLoop()` extraction, `resumeCapability()`, new `action:'continue'` dispatch). `S-ARCH-DURABLE-LOOP-02b` — frontend (`MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`'s shared `resolveInProgress()` helper wired into `callCapability()`'s 9 call sites and `resolveConfirmation()`'s 1). Both independently re-verified by the design session, not accepted from either coding report alone — session `a`: `git diff` confirmed zero lines changed inside `runCapability()`, direct Supabase re-query confirmed the forced-checkpoint row reached `status:complete` and zero rows leaked from the fast-path test (real credentials had to be sourced separately from the coding agent's sandbox, which lacked them). Session `b`: `git diff` confirmed the change scoped to exactly the new helper + 2 one-line edits. **Live regression check against the real deployed dev URL** (not the coding session's isolated `vite`, which can't reach real serverless functions): the exact original incident question, real end-to-end run — routing (3.1s) → answered (26.9s) → Owen's Quality Gate (58.2s, within 2s of the old ceiling on its own) → display formatting (28.1s) — 4 real calls, all 200, real rendered answer, zero errors. Stayed on the fast path this run; checkpoint/resume itself already proven via session `a`'s forced-budget test. The 58.2s gate hop is live evidence the already-logged `AA-124` "decision needed" item (Owen's retry-to-Marcus chain) is a present risk, not theoretical — still open, unscheduled. | ✅ Done | S-ARCH-DURABLE-LOOP-02a (v6.1.1, 99b2fc4/76ee295) / 02b (v6.1.2, 34d8e5c) |
| AA-147 | **Formerly tracked informally as `task_a8249f13`; given its own row 2026-07-08 at the start of `S-ARCH-SCHEMA-GUARD-01-design` (Backlog Capture rule), tagged `Continuity` under the new Type taxonomy.** `buildCallBody()`'s `tool_choice` is forced to `'auto'` whenever a capability has both a JSON schema and `can_request_help: true` — necessary so the model can legitimately choose `request_help`/`delegate_to_agent` instead of answering directly, but `'auto'` also let the model satisfy "call a tool" by calling its own schema tool with an empty or partial input object. `parseModelTurn()` only checked that *a* `tool_use` block existed, never that its `input` held the schema's required fields; the unvalidated input flowed straight through `sendRequest()` into a deliverable with missing/undefined content, no error anywhere. **Confirmed blast radius via live Supabase query before speccing the fix:** 10 Intent Skill Profiles combine `can_request_help`+schema; 2 (`ci-answer-display-intent`, `hyp-hypothesis-test-display-intent`) were already protected by `AA-142`'s guard, leaving 8 unprotected — including `ci-answer-intent` (Marcus's main Q&A path) and `qg-review-intent` (Owen's Proofreader, runs on every Q&A/hypothesis turn), the widest-blast-radius unfixed gap in the `AA-126`/`AA-142`/`AA-144`/`AA-141`/`AA-145`/`AA-135` continuity bug family. **Fix, 1 file:** `parseModelTurn()` now takes a `schemaTool` param and throws (naming the missing keys) when the model's own schema-tool call is missing required fields; both `callModel()` call sites updated to pass it through. Reuses `callModel()`'s existing retry-once-then-throw path unchanged — no new retry logic. `request_help`/`delegate_to_agent` calls are explicitly exempt. Node test 6/6 PASS, file deleted before commit. **Independently re-verified by the design session, not accepted from the coding report alone:** `git show` confirmed the diff scoped to exactly the 1 file, 21 insertions/3 deletions, matching the kickoff precisely. Live Q&A round trip against the real deployed dev URL (coding session's own test, one pass per `STANDARDS.md`'s live-test-once rule) exercised both newly-protected high-traffic intents at once — `ci-answer-intent` (Answered, confidence_tier: Sourced, 25.8s) and `qg-review-intent` (Owen's guardrail/eval: pass, 9.4s) — full loop completed cleanly, all 4 `/api/capabilities/execute` calls 200, zero console errors. One display artifact observed (Riley's HTML rendering as raw text) was confirmed pre-existing and unrelated (`AA-135`/`AA-146` territory — `schemaTool` is null on the `html` output-type path, so this fix's new branch never engages there), not a regression. Kickoff: `docs/kickoffs/v6.1.19-S-ARCH-SCHEMA-GUARD-01-aa147-tool-choice-schema-validation.md`. | ✅ Done | S-ARCH-SCHEMA-GUARD-01-design/S-ARCH-SCHEMA-GUARD-01 (v6.1.19, 30abdd9) |
| AA-126 | **Silent content-loss bug on the `delegate_to_agent` hand-off — root-caused and fixed 2026-07-07, found by the design session investigating John's "haven't seen Column 2 charts in several sessions" question.** Root cause: `DELEGATE_TO_AGENT_TOOL`'s `task` field (`request-receivable.js`) is a plain string by design — the delegating agent (Priya) had to paraphrase her own structured `supports`/`complicates`/`consider` content (with real citation UUIDs) into free text for that one field. Alex's `intelligence-review-format` requires every claim, especially `key_data_points`' citations, to trace back to real given text — fed only the paraphrase, his call returned little/nothing, and `execute.js`'s `{...delegateResult.content}` spread silently absorbed the emptiness into a hollow card, no error surfaced. **Confirmed the visualization mechanism itself (`AA-117`/`MI-13`) was never broken** — a direct call to Alex with real structured input produced a correct card and chart; only the real production hand-off path lost fidelity. **Fix, 1 file, 2 tasks (`api/capabilities/execute.js`):** threaded the delegator's own `task_context` through `runLoop()`/`runCapability()`/`resumeCapability()` (the latter passes `null`, an accepted gap matching `AA-139`'s existing non-persisted-field precedent since `durable_hops` doesn't store it) and merged it into the delegate's `task_context` at the `delegate_to_agent` branch (`{...task_context, delegation_task: task}`) instead of forwarding `task` alone; added a loud-failure guard that throws when a final delegation returns empty content, naming the delegate and any guardrail violations. Harness-generic — no agent_id/capability_slug hardcoded anywhere, confirmed against `ARCHITECTURE.md` §19d's sniff test before the kickoff was written. Node test 12/12 PASS (one transient empty-content failure correctly caught loudly by the new Task 2 guard on first run, consistent with the separately-tracked `task_a8249f13` tool-choice bug — not this fix; a clean rerun passed all 12). **Independently re-verified by the design session, not accepted from the coding report alone:** `git show` confirmed the diff scoped to exactly the 1 file, 33 insertions/4 deletions, matching the kickoff precisely; two fresh, independent live calls against the deployed dev URL (not the coding session's own test) — the exact previously-broken Priya→Michelle→Alex hand-off now returns a full card (`headline`/`supports`/`complicates`/`key_data_points`) and a correctly-populated `visualization` (`bar_pair`, Nordholm co-op utilization 55% vs 90% target — the literal symptom John reported); Marcus's Q&A display hand-off (`ci-answer-display-intent`, a second real consumer of this same code) independently re-confirmed unaffected. **Live pixel-level Column 2 verification accepted on indirect evidence, documented explicitly:** this worktree had no `.env.local`, and this project's local `vite dev` cannot reach real serverless functions regardless (same recurring limitation as `AA-127`/`128`/`130`/`131`/`MI-15`) — the two independent live API calls against the actual deployed function are the direct evidence instead, exercising the identical code path the UI calls. Ran in its own git worktree (`session/aa126-delegation-fidelity`), zero collisions. | AA-148 | **Live-reproduced 2026-07-08 from John's own report** (a Q&A answer that was narration about a delegation instead of real content, with Michelle/Alex missing from Agent Routing) — confirmed via `claude-in-chrome` against the real dev URL and cross-checked against `ai_activity_log`/`durable_hops`, not accepted from the screenshot alone. **Not a recurrence of `AA-142`** — `delegation_required: true` was correctly set and real delegation genuinely occurred (Michelle really selected a Display agent, the agent really generated real output). Two compounding gaps, both confirmed live: (1) `ci-answer-display-intent`/`hyp-hypothesis-test-display-intent` left `is_final` as model judgment on `delegate_to_agent` even though both intents' own method text guarantees there's never a legitimate `false` case (a Format Skill never changes what it's handed) — when the model omitted `is_final:true`, Marcus got a superfluous wrap-up turn that narrated instead of relaying the delegate's real content, and `display.selection`/`display_agent_id` never populated (why Michelle/Alex were invisible in Agent Routing — a downstream symptom, not a separate logging bug). (2) That narration should still have been caught by `AA-142`'s guard, but `resumeCapability()` hardcoded `delegationRequired: false` on any request that checkpoints mid-flight — an already-documented `AA-142` "accepted gap" that live `durable_hops` data proved was not hypothetical: this exact reproduction's request checkpointed and resumed mid-flight, silently disabling the guard for the rest of the request. **Fix, 2 code files + 1 Supabase migration:** `execute.js`'s terminal-delegation branch changed to `if (delegationRequired || turn.tool_input.is_final === true)` — no legitimate `false` case exists once a capability's entire job is delegation, same logic `delegation_required` itself already established (§19d sniff test resolved explicitly in the kickoff, not just cited); `durable_hops` gained a `delegation_required` column, threaded through `checkpointAndReturn()`/`createDurableHopRow()`/`resumeCapability()` so a resumed chain keeps the guard active. No data/prompt edit needed — both intents' `skill_profiles.traits.delegation_required` were already correct. Node test 6/6 PASS, including a **deterministic** forced checkpoint/resume case via the existing `__setTestBudgetMs()` test hook (not dependent on random narration timing, unlike `AA-142`'s own test). **Independently re-verified by the design session, not accepted from the coding report alone:** the coding session's own live QA (all 6 Manual QA items) exercised a real production checkpoint/resume for real (`durable_hops` row `d4d18e19...`, two `depth1` turns 12.8s apart) and confirmed the correct final result. The design session then ran its own separate live spot-check via `claude-in-chrome` against the same dev URL: run 1 hit a genuine, already-documented, pre-existing intermittent case (`AA-144`/`AA-145`'s "empty content from `alex`/`screen-controls`/`qa-answer-format`, correctly caught by the unweakened guard") — surfaced as a clean error now instead of being masked by narration, which is the fix working correctly, not a regression; run 2 succeeded cleanly, Michelle→Riley Torres delegation with full real HTML-formatted content (citations, driver table) and correct 7-event Agent Routing attribution. Patched (John's explicit call): `refreshHopEstimateCache()` now paginates in pages of 1000 until exhausted. Live-verified independently, not accepted from either coding report alone: `data-room-custody`/null now returns real p99+margin (51168ms), `ci-routing-intent` unchanged (no regression). Does not reduce Owen's chain length or the underlying 60s ceiling — a threshold-accuracy fix, not a chain-shortening one. | ✅ Done | S-ARCH-HOP-BUDGET-01-design/S-ARCH-HOP-BUDGET-01 (v6.3.19, `971dd06`) · S-ARCH-HOP-BUDGET-01-patch-design/S-ARCH-HOP-BUDGET-01-patch (v6.3.21, `80478c4`) |

## HARNESS — HAR
| ID | Feature | Status | Session |
|----|---------|--------|---------|
| HAR-04 | **Split 2026-07-17 from legacy `AA-69` (`plan.js` chain-timeout finding) — this half is the shared-Harness, CI-relevant piece; `AA-69` itself now covers only `plan.js`'s separate, PM-specific missing-checkpoint gap (`docs/FEATURES-LATER.md`).** `request-receivable.js`'s `callModel()`/`sendRequest()` made up to three sequential Anthropic calls (main call, parse-failure retry, guardrails check), each with an independent fixed timeout (55s/55s/20s), none aware of the caller's actual remaining `maxDuration` budget — a hop needing a retry could internally consume ~110s+ against a 60s ceiling, a silent Vercel 504 with zero trace. Confirmed relevant to Channel Intelligence, not just `plan.js`: `execute.js`'s `runLoop()` already checks estimated remaining time before each hop (`AA-196`'s p99-based estimate), but that check doesn't cover the tail case where `callModel()`'s own internal retry doubles a hop's call time. **Fix, 2 files, reuses `execute.js`'s existing `_deadline` pattern rather than inventing a new one:** `callModel()`/`sendRequest()` gained an optional `deadline` param, bounding each internal call to real remaining time and failing fast with a clear error below a minimum-viable-call floor instead of attempting a doomed call; `execute.js`'s two existing call sites now thread its already-computed `deadline` through. Byte-identical for every caller that omits `deadline` (`plan.js`, `confirmation.js` — their own risk is `AA-69`, untouched here). Node test 7/7 PASS (mocked, no network), build clean. **Independently re-verified by the design session, not accepted from the coding report alone:** confirmed both commits (`5422f94` fix, `627e0be` — removed a stray test file the coding session forgot to delete before committing, per `STANDARDS.md` Section 4) landed on `dev` with exactly the claimed diffs; ran a fresh live spot-check against the real deployed dev endpoint (`channel-intelligence`/`ci-answer-intent`/Marcus), confirming a clean 200 in 11.6s with real content, consistent with the coding session's own reported 5/5 forced-retry + 3/3 fast-path + 1/1 `plan.js`-unaffected results. Kickoff: `docs/kickoffs/v6.3.49-HAR-04-callmodel-deadline-aware-timeouts.md`. | ✅ Done | S-SES003-TSR-design/S-HAR-04 (v6.3.49, `5422f94`/`627e0be`) |
