| AA-173 | **[Continuity]** **New, found 2026-07-09 (overnight autonomous session), John's explicit ask: draft the 20 business questions Apple's VP of Channel Sales is most likely to ask, run them through the real Q&A pipeline (not a shortcut -- exact `ci-answer-intent` → `qg-review-intent` → `ci-answer-display-intent` sequence `runQaWithQualityGate()` uses), grade for truthfulness, and root-cause any failure as harness/loop/agent/data.** Questions grounded in the actual seeded `the_library` content (pulled live first, 21 active rows across 10 partner scenarios/9 GEOs/3 GEO briefings/7 market-benchmark-and-program-structure references), covering every partner scenario, all 3 GEO briefings, both industry benchmarks, and 3 macro/program-structure references, plus one deliberate hallucination trap (a South Korea partner that does not exist in the Data Room). **Result: 17/20 (85%) completed cleanly on the first pass with real citations, appropriate confidence tiers, and no fabrication** — full list and answers in this session's transcript. The hallucination trap (Q20 first pass) was handled correctly: the model explicitly stated it had no South Korea data rather than inventing an answer. **3 failures investigated in full, none were a silent wrong-answer — every one was either a correct safety block or a truncation, never a fabricated answer reaching the user:**
1. **Meridian Electronics digital shelf compliance question** — `qg-review-intent` correctly blocked (`synthesized_as_fact`/`citation_missing`) because `ci-answer-intent` stated specific numbers (62% compliance, 90% target, 9.58% YoY) without sourcing discipline tight enough per-claim. Confirmed by direct re-test on 3 fresh attempts: still blocks every time — this is Owen doing his job correctly, not a bug, though it does mean this specific question currently always ends in a graceful "let me research this further" reply (via Sam's `pipeline-triage` escalation) rather than a usable answer.
2. **Cross-partner "biggest at-risk accounts globally" question** — the hardest test in the set. Root-caused two layered causes, not one: (a) `ci-answer-intent`'s `max_tokens: 1500` was too tight for genuinely broad multi-entity synthesis -- the raw failing payload showed the `answer` field truncated mid-word before ever reaching the trailing required fields (`needs_review`/`review_reason`/`citations`), the same failure shape as `AA-151` but on Marcus's own core answer schema, not just `agent-selection-intent`. **Fixed:** `max_tokens` 1500→3000 (Supabase-only, same precedent as `AA-161`). **Measured:** 1 of 3 post-fix re-test attempts completed cleanly where all pre-fix attempts truncated -- real improvement, not fully resolved. (b) Even with more budget, Marcus tends to assert precise-sounding statistics across multiple partners with looser citation grounding than a single-partner question -- likely connected to RAG's fixed `match_count: 5` retrieval breadth being insufficient for a question that legitimately spans up to 10 partner scenarios at once. **Not fixed tonight** -- this is a retrieval-breadth/prompt-hedging design question (should broad synthesis questions get a larger `match_count`, or should `ci-answer-intent` be instructed to explicitly hedge/generalize rather than assert unsupported specifics when covering many entities), not a narrow bug, and touches the shared `embedAndSearch()`/RAG path every knowledge-backed capability uses -- too broad a change to make unverified this late. Recommended as a follow-up design session.
3. **South Korea hallucination-trap question** — first-pass result (in the original 20-question run) was clean and correct. Adversarial re-testing (3 more attempts) found it does NOT always land clean: `ci-answer-intent` sometimes asserts a specific unsupported claim while explaining the absence (e.g. "Eleanor's catalog review confirms no entry exists for South Korea" without a citation for that claim) and gets correctly blocked by `qg-review-intent` before it can retry. Separately, one re-test attempt's `gate.final_answer` (after a successful retry on the cross-partner question, not this one) showed the regenerated text bled internal meta-commentary ("The candidate answer's claims... cannot be sourced") into what the user would see -- confirmed this is a retry-instruction quality gap in `qg-review-intent`'s own `delegate_to_agent` call, not a rendering bug. **Fixed:** added explicit "RETRY INSTRUCTION QUALITY" guidance to `qg-review-intent`'s `method` -- retry requests must ask for a fresh standalone answer, never invite the agent to reference/critique its own rejected draft; also added "an incorrect confidence_tier label on an otherwise sound answer" as an explicit example of a fixable-via-retry case (previously only "missing citation" was given as an example, which may have made Owen under-use his own retry discretion). **Measured:** 0/9 re-test attempts (across all 3 investigated questions) showed leaked meta-commentary post-fix -- no regression, but most attempts didn't reach a retry at all (blocked outright on the first answer), so this fix's positive effect is not yet strongly confirmed, only that it didn't make anything worse.

**Verdict against harness/loop/agent/data, per John's explicit framework:** harness is sound (17/20 clean completions, zero crashes/hangs/timeouts across the full 20-question run plus 9 re-test attempts). The agent delegation loop and quality-gate blocking behaved exactly as designed on every edge case, including ones this session didn't anticipate -- this is the platform's safety mechanism working, not failing. The one real, generalizable code-level bug found and fixed was `ci-answer-intent`'s `max_tokens` truncation (same failure family as `AA-151`, now confirmed to extend beyond `agent-selection-intent`). The residual gap is agent-level (Marcus's citation-precision discipline on broad/thin-data questions) with a data-layer contributor (RAG retrieval breadth) -- recommend a dedicated design session on RAG `match_count` tuning for multi-entity questions before the next reliability push. | ✅ Done | Overnight session 2026-07-08/09 (Supabase-only: `ci-answer-intent.max_tokens`, `qg-review-intent.method`) → **`S-ARCH-RAG-BREADTH-01`** (v6.2.32, `21486ca`, 2026-07-14: `db-assembly.js` threads optional `traits.match_count` override, `ci-knowledge` widened 5→10; live-verified matchCount 10 delivers real chunks 5→10 for a broad synthesis question via `queryLibrary()` and the full `assemblePrompt→enrichPrompt` chain, full `runQaWithQualityGate()` pipeline completes cleanly, narrow single-partner question unaffected — full detail: `CLAUDE-STATE.md` "Last 3 sessions") |
| AA-174 | **[Continuity]** **New, found 2026-07-09 (overnight autonomous session), John's follow-up ask: what other questions should be tested, specifically self-introspection/meta/architecture questions (e.g. "how many agents exist," "has any prediction come true," "is this really a loop-and-harness platform, or hardcoded").** A genuinely different test axis than `AA-173` — not "is the answer grounded in Data Room content" but "does the agent know the boundary of its own competence, or does it fabricate when asked something it has no mechanism to know." the other 5 either got hard-blocked-then-escalated after an attempted answer, or (worse) exhibited two distinct failure shapes that passed the guardrail undetected: **scope drift** (asked for cross-session pattern-mining it doesn't have, answered a different adjacent question instead of admitting the gap) and a **fabricated tool-use narrative** (invented an entire `request_help`/delegation to a real agent, "Dan Bingham," that never happened, to answer a competitive-positioning question). **Root cause:** `ci-answer-intent`'s method had explicit handling for catalog/inventory questions (delegate to Eleanor) but no guidance at all for "this question isn't about Data Room content, it's about the platform itself" — so behavior on that class was inconsistent, driven by luck rather than instruction. **Fixed (Supabase-only, `ci-answer-intent.method`):** added an explicit SCOPE BOUNDARY section modeled directly on the one question that already behaved correctly — recognize platform/architecture/operational-history questions on the first attempt the same instinct already used for catalog questions, but never delegate or invent a tool-use event for them (there is no agent who owns "explain the platform to a user"); decline directly and briefly with `confidence_tier: na`; explicitly forbidden from answering an adjacent question instead of admitting a capability gap. **First re-test round (7 questions) found the fix worked exactly as intended for content — all 7 answers now used identical, correct, honest decline language, zero fabricated delegations, zero scope drift — but exposed a second, distinct bug:** 4 of 7 still got hard-blocked by `qg-review-intent`'s `missing_confidence` rule, which reads literally as "block any claim about the platform itself without a sourced/inferred/synthesized tier" — Owen was inconsistently applying this to legitimate `na`-tagged capability-scope declines (blocked 4, passed 3, on near-identical content), a rules conflict between his own guardrail and `ci-answer-intent`'s own documented legitimate-`na` cases. **Fixed (Supabase-only, `qg-review-intent.method`):** added an explicit carve-out distinguishing a capability-scope decline (legitimate `na`, not a factual assertion) from a genuine unsourced platform-fact claim (still correctly blocked). **Final re-test (the 4 that had failed): 4/4 clean, `confidence_tier: na`, no escalation needed. The dominant lever really was reliability (silent schema-validation failures forcing retries/hangs), exactly as this session's fixes targeted — not request-budget architecture. See `AA-157` (renumbered from `AA-154` after that ID/version collided with an unrelated concurrent session, `S-ARCH-TEMPERATURE-01`). **`S-ARCH-RETRY-CATCH-01` (bug 1) confirmed shipped and working as designed** — coding session's own live check: 4/5 pass, the 1 failure came back as the intended graceful 422, zero raw 500s. Design session independently re-verified twice more after all four concurrent sessions' changes landed on `dev` (8 combined attempts): zero raw 500s across all 8 — the fix holds. But the underlying validation-failure rate on this call is unchanged from the 40-60% baseline, exactly as the kickoff predicted ("this fix changes HOW a still-failing case is reported, not whether it fails") — 3 of the 8 attempts still failed with the graceful 422. **New, distinct finding from this same re-verification:** 1 of the 8 attempts hit a 60s+ HTTP 504 gateway timeout instead of any parse-related error — a third failure mode, likely related to `AA-124`'s already-documented "~50s of real LLM work inside a 60s-budgeted request" finding, not a regression from today's fixes. **Bottom line for ship-readiness:** raw crashes are gone, but Priya's chart-generating call still fails roughly 40-50% of the time in clean live testing (parse-validation failures plus occasional gateway timeouts) — this now looks like an underlying model-reliability/request-budget ceiling on this specific multi-hop intent, not a remaining harness bug to patch. Recommend John decide the strategy (schema simplification, forcing `tool_choice` on retry, restructuring the intent, or a request-budget fix) rather than another blind fix-and-verify cycle. **`AA-157` (bug 2) closed out and archived** (`docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`, v6.1.27, `60c1525`) — Node test 2/2, build clean, 0/3 regression on Owen, independent spot-check clean. Its own QA found `AA-158` (`execute.js` drops `.detail` from every error response, blocking anyone from actually seeing the 422/504 detail this fix and `AA-151` bug 1 now produce) — **`AA-158` has since shipped and been archived** (`docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`, v6.1.29), so `.detail` is now visible on the next occurrence of either failure mode. **Independently re-confirmed 2026-07-08 (design session, 6-run live test across all 3 real Apple demo seed questions, post-`AA-157`/`AA-158`/`AA-149` landing):** this bug is not scoped to Priya's chart path alone — the identical `agent-selection-intent`/`recommended_agent_id` signature also hit Marcus's real Q&A display hand-off (2 of 6 runs, EMEA and Vietnam seed questions), confirming the same root cause blocks the core Q&A feature, not just Column 2 charts. Full 6-run breakdown: 2 failed with this signature, 2 completed but hit `AA-153`'s raw-HTML fallback (Riley), 1 completed with a new unconfirmed anomaly (see `AA-160`), and only 1 of 6 was a fully clean card end-to-end. Raises the ship-readiness stakes beyond charts to the Q&A path itself — same strategy decision needed, now with broader scope. **Strategy decided 2026-07-08 (John's call, design session `aa151-agent-selection-budget-0708`):** live re-verification (5 attempts) isolated the residual failure to `agent-selection-intent` alone — `intelligence-review-format`'s signature no longer reproduced, likely already fixed by `AA-161`'s live budget bump. `tool_choice`-forcing ruled out by direct Supabase check (`can_request_help: null`, so `tool_choice` is already forced to the schema tool on both attempt and retry). Fix shipped: `max_tokens` bump 1200→2200, no A/B measurement, per kickoff `docs/kickoffs/v6.1.31-S-ARCH-AGENT-SELECT-BUDGET-01-aa151-agent-selection-max-tokens.md`. **Fix did NOT resolve the bug (2026-07-08):** coding session's own honest live re-test, 4/5 PASS, same `recommended_agent_id` omission signature — no measurable change from this design session's own pre-fix baseline (also 4/5). Per John's new standing rule ([[feedback-no-blind-fixes]] — no fix ships without root-cause measurement first, going forward), rather than guess a third fix, root-caused why no real token data existed to check the theory against, found and shipped `AI-47` (two `ai_activity_log` write-site bugs, see that row / `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`), then re-ran the repro (6 fresh attempts, 2/6 PASS) with real telemetry finally flowing. **Budget theory now ruled out by real measurement, not just unproven:** all 5 captured `agent-selection-intent` calls during that run show `output_tokens` 565-1062 — comfortably under the 2200 cap, mostly under the *old* 1200 cap too, no sign of at-cap truncation on any of them. This is consistent with (and explains) the budget bump producing zero measurable change. Root cause still open — next lever is schema simplification or restructuring `agent-selection-intent`, not more budget. **Per John's go-ahead (2026-07-08), next step is seeing the model's actual raw output on a failing call, not guessing at a third fix.** Confirmed by direct code read: `parseModelTurn()` already has the raw partial `tool_input` in scope when it detects the missing field, but discards it — the thrown error only ever carried the missing-key list. Kickoff written to surface it: `docs/kickoffs/v6.1.37-S-ARCH-RAW-INPUT-DIAGNOSTIC-01-aa151-raw-input-visibility.md` (1 file, reuses `AA-158`'s existing `.detail` passthrough, no new mechanism). **`S-ARCH-RAW-INPUT-DIAGNOSTIC-01` executed 2026-07-08/09 (overnight autonomous root-cause session, John's explicit go-ahead to act without waiting for confirmation):** live repro against dev, 4th attempt reproduced the 422. Raw `tool_input` captured verbatim: the model wrote a full, valid `candidates` array (3 real candidates) and a `reasoning` string, but partway through — well past the schema's `maxLength: 300` (the actual string ran ~900 chars) — the `reasoning` string's own text trails into a literal, non-JSON fragment: `...not a machine-readable card structure.</reasoning>\n<parameter name="recommended_agent_id">alex` — then the JSON resumes with `"recommended_capability_slug":"screen-controls"` as the next real key. **Root cause: the model never emits `recommended_agent_id` as its own top-level JSON key at all** — it writes the value (`alex`) as trailing literal text *inside* the `reasoning` string, using invented XML-ish syntax (`</reasoning>`, `<parameter name="...">`) that appears nowhere in this codebase's own prompts (grepped `skill_profiles`/`db-assembly.js`/every prompt-building file for `parameter name=`/`invoke name=` — zero matches, ruling out a leaked-example theory). This is a state-transition failure: after generating a long, unconstrained-in-practice `reasoning` narrative, the model loses track of the schema's remaining required keys. **Fix applied (Supabase `skill_profiles.traits`/`guardrails` for `agent-selection-intent`, no code file):** reordered the schema's `required` array to `[recommended_agent_id, recommended_capability_slug, candidates, reasoning]`, added per-field descriptions instructing the model to write the recommendation FIRST and treat `reasoning` as a short post-hoc justification (not a chain-of-thought that precedes the decision), tightened `reasoning.maxLength` 300→400, and added a matching `guardrails.must` entry. **Caveat found while applying this:** Postgres `jsonb` does not preserve object key insertion order (it reorders by key length then lexically, confirmed by reading the row back) — so the `properties` object's write-order has zero effect on what Anthropic sees; the `required` array (a JSON array, order-preserving) and the explicit natural-language instructions are the only real levers here, not property order. **Measured, not assumed (per [[feedback-no-blind-fixes]]):** isolated direct `agent-selection-intent` calls, pre-fix baseline 1/10 failed (10%); post-fix 0/15 (0%). Full production chain (`hyp-hypothesis-test-intent` → `hyp-hypothesis-test-display-intent`, the exact path measured at 40-60% failure historically): post-fix 0/8 clean completions, 42-59s each. Strong, measured improvement — not yet proven at zero failure rate (small samples), continued monitoring recommended, but this is very likely the dominant fix for both the Q&A path and Column 2 charts. **New finding surfaced by this same test, not yet resolved:** all 8 clean full-chain completions returned `visualization: undefined` — Column 2 charts still are not populating even when the call succeeds cleanly; this is now decoupled from AA-151 (reliability) and needs its own investigation, see `AA-151b` below. | ✅ Done | `S-ARCH-RETRY-CATCH-01` (v6.1.25, done) + `S-ARCH-RETRY-DIAGNOSTICS-01` (v6.1.27, done) + `S-ARCH-EXECUTE-DETAIL-01` (v6.1.29, done) + `S-ARCH-AGENT-SELECT-BUDGET-01` (v6.1.31, shipped, did not resolve) + `AI-47` (v6.1.33, done, unblocked real diagnosis) + `S-ARCH-RAW-INPUT-DIAGNOSTIC-01` (v6.1.37, done) + overnight session 2026-07-08/09 (Supabase-only schema/instruction fix, measured 0/15 isolated + 0/8 full-chain post-fix) |
| AA-151b | **[Continuity]** **New, found 2026-07-08/09 during the overnight `AA-151` fix's own post-fix measurement.** All 8 of 8 clean full-chain completions of `hyp-hypothesis-test-display-intent` (Priya, real seed-question content, post-`AA-151` schema fix) returned no `visualization` field at all — Column 2 charts still do not render even on a fully successful, non-erroring call. Not yet root-caused — candidates to check: (1) whether `intelligence-review-format`'s schema still lists `visualization` as truly optional and the model is simply choosing not to populate it now that the failure-prone required fields succeed reliably, (2) whether `AA-161`'s `max_tokens` 1500→3000 bump actually shipped/landed on the right skill profile row, (3) whether the frontend (`MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`) is looking for the field under a different key/shape than what's actually returned. Needs direct investigation before Apple demo — charts are a stated ship requirement. **Investigated same session, 2026-07-08/09:** direct isolated call to `intelligence-review-format` (Alex) with content deliberately containing an unambiguous single-metric current-vs-target pair (55% current / 80% target, matching the schema's own worked example) produced `visualization` correctly on 5/5 attempts — the mechanism itself is not broken, and `AA-161`'s `max_tokens: 3000` bump is confirmed live in the DB. Inspected Priya's actual raw analysis output from the same full-chain test that returned 0/8 visualizations: her real `supports`/`complicates`/`consider` text for this seed question is genuinely qualitative (store-level segmentation, a 42-day-vs-30-45-day industry benchmark *range*, not a single target; `confidence: "low"`) and never restates the original 55%/80% headline numbers at all — Alex's conservative "omit rather than invent" instruction (already in the schema) is very likely doing exactly what it's supposed to do here, not failing. **Reframed: likely not a bug** — probably a property of this specific seed question's real analysis output (low-confidence, comparison-averse reasoning), not a code/prompt defect. Needs confirmation against the actual 3 Apple demo seed questions in this session's end-to-end pass (task tracked separately) before closing out either way — if a seed question whose flagged answer already states a clean current/target pair still produces no chart, that would be a real bug; if it's consistently qualitative content correctly declining to chart, no fix is needed. **Confirmed against real seed-question content, 2026-07-08/09:** re-ran the real EMEA seed question's full hypothesis-test chain 4 more times post-`AA-151` fix — 4/4 clean (0% failure), and 1/4 (25%) produced a real chart (`"headline":"Approval bottleneck explains part, not all, of Nordholm's 25..."`). **Closed out: confirmed not a bug.** The mechanism works and does chart real seed-question content when Priya's own analysis restates a clear current/target pair — it just doesn't do so on every single run, because her analytical reasoning is genuinely non-deterministic run to run (sometimes emphasizing the numeric comparison, sometimes emphasizing qualitative segmentation instead). Charts are also only reachable via the explicit "Generate Hypotheses" flow, not the direct Q&A path the 3 example seed questions route to by default (`ci-routing-intent` classified all 3 as `qa`, never `theory`) — a user must explicitly submit a hypothesis to see a chart at all. Recommend, as a separate non-urgent follow-up: if more consistent charting is wanted, tighten `hyp-hypothesis-test-intent`'s own instructions to more reliably restate a current/target pair when the underlying Data Room content supports one — that's a quality/consistency enhancement, not a bug fix. **Follow-up done same session, 2026-07-08/09 (John asked directly: "what question is guaranteed to produce a chart?"):** added an explicit "CHART-CARRYING REQUIREMENT" to `hyp-hypothesis-test-intent`'s `method` — when `flagged_question`/`flagged_answer` already state a concrete current-vs-target pair for one metric, Priya must restate the exact numbers verbatim in `supports`, not just paraphrase the gap qualitatively. Also pinned `temperature: 0` on both `hyp-hypothesis-test-intent` and `intelligence-review-format` (previously null on both — the only 2 remaining unpinned intents in this whole chain). **Measured: 12/12 clean chart appearances in the two verification batches run immediately after both fixes** (vs. 1/4 before any fix, 3/5 after the method fix alone but before the temperature pin) — on the real EMEA seed question, submitting the hypothesis "The 55% co-op utilization gap is driven primarily by an internal approval-process bottleneck..." Recommended answer for a guaranteed-chart demo moment: ask the EMEA co-op question, then submit that same (or a very similarly-worded) hypothesis via Generate Hypotheses. Not mathematically 100% (still a live model call, not fully deterministic even at temperature 0), but odds are now very high, up from ~25%. | ✅ Done | Overnight session 2026-07-08/09 — Supabase only (`method` + `temperature` columns on 2 skill profiles), no code file |
| AA-168 | **[Continuity]** **New, found 2026-07-08 in a live design-session investigation of `AA-162`'s real-world reliability, after John reported the catalog-question chat flow failing live in the MI screen.** Two distinct root causes, both live-confirmed by direct reproduction against the real deployed pipeline, not guessed:

**1. `AA-163`'s new `confidence_tier: na` rubric (v6.1.34) collides with `AA-162`'s synchronous delegation pattern.** The rubric legitimizes `na` for "a status update about an in-progress hand-off (e.g. 'delegated this to X')" — written for a genuinely async case, but Marcus's `ci-answer-intent` → Eleanor Voss delegation (`AA-162`) is not actually async: `delegate_to_agent` returns synchronously as a tool_result before Marcus's next turn, so by the time he writes his final answer, Eleanor's real catalog data is already sitting in his own conversation history. Confirmed live, 4/4 fresh attempts against current `dev`: Marcus consistently writes narration ("I've routed this to Eleanor Voss... She is retrieving...") instead of using her actual returned data, tags it `na`, and Owen's guardrail passes it through with the reasoning *"The 'na' confidence tier is legitimate for a status update about an in-progress hand-off"* (verbatim quote from a live `qg-review-intent` response) — the rubric is being followed exactly as written; this is a rubric-wording gap, not a model failure. **Fix:** refine the "in-progress hand-off" carve-out on both `ci-answer-intent`'s schema description and `qg-review-intent`'s method to exclude the case where the delegate's response has already been received as a tool_result — `na` is only legitimate while genuinely still waiting, never after the real answer is already in hand. Supabase-only, 2 rows (mirrors `AA-163`'s own shape).

**2. A separate, intermittent content-corruption bug in `execute.js`'s `is_final` delegation branch.** `runLoop()`'s `finalResult` construction (`api/capabilities/execute.js` ~line 392, `...delegateResult.content`) assumes every `is_final` delegate returns `content` as a JS object. For a JSON-schema delegate (e.g. Alex/`qa-answer-format`) this is correct. For any plain-text/HTML-output delegate (e.g. Riley/`html-display`, `output_type: "html"`, whose `sendRequest()` content is legitimately a raw string) spreading a string produces the classic JS footgun: a character-indexed object (`{0:"<",1:"h",2:"t",...}`) instead of the real text. Confirmed live: reproduced the exact corrupted shape when Michelle picked Riley for the Display hand-off; a separate run where Michelle picked Alex came back clean — confirms this is intermittent, gated entirely by which display agent gets selected each time. Not a regression from `AA-162` — a pre-existing structural gap that `AA-162`'s new delegation path is now exercising for the first time. **Fix:** guard the spread — `typeof delegateResult.content === 'object' ? delegateResult.content : { text: delegateResult.content }` — plus a matching frontend recognition of the `{text: ...}` shape in `MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`'s `runQaWithQualityGate()`, mirroring the existing `typeof display === "string"` fallback already there (`AA-137`).

**Relationship to `AA-166` (temperature, already logged) — complementary, not the same bug.** `AA-166`'s own repro showed explicit schema-validation errors (missing required fields) — a different failure signature than this row's clean-but-wrong narration answers. Recommend one session covering `AA-166` + both fixes above for a genuinely reliable catalog-question path: temperature pinning reduces malformed/retry-failing calls, fix #1 stops the model from narrating instead of answering even when calls succeed cleanly, fix #2 prevents corruption on the subset of successful runs that pick an HTML-output display agent. Scope fits one session: Supabase (na rubric ×2 rows + temperature ×2 rows) + 2 code files (`execute.js`, `MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`) — within the 3-file/4-task session limit. **All three addressed 2026-07-08/09 (overnight autonomous session):** **Bug 1 (na-rubric collision) fixed** — refined `ci-answer-intent`'s `confidence_tier` schema description and `qg-review-intent`'s `method` to explicitly exclude an already-completed synchronous delegation from the "in-progress hand-off" na carve-out, and to instruct Owen to block a narration-only na answer as a `synthesized_as_fact`-class violation. **Measured: 5/5 live calls to the real catalog question ("What data and categories do you actually have in the Data Room for our Nordholm account?") now return `confidence_tier: sourced` with real citations, zero narration, zero na** (direct call to `ci-answer-intent`; full chain through Owen's review not separately isolated but the rubric text is symmetric on both sides). **Bug 2 (content-spread corruption) found ALREADY FIXED by an earlier same-day session, not by this one** — direct code read of `buildFinalDelegationResult()` (`execute.js` ~line 198, this function did not exist under that name at the time `AA-168` was originally written; the code has since been extracted/refactored) shows the `isPlainObject` guard from `S-ARCH-STRING-CONTENT-01`/`AA-135` (commit `07ee532`, same day, earlier) already prevents spreading a string as character-indexed junk, and `MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`'s `runQaWithQualityGate()`/`runHypothesisTest()` both already have the matching `typeof display.content === "string"` fallback. This row's bug-2 description was accurate when written but is now stale — no code change was needed here. **AA-166 (temperature) fixed** — `library-catalog-intent` and `ci-answer-display-intent` both pinned to `temperature: 0` in Supabase (previously null), matching `ci-answer-intent`/`qg-review-intent`'s existing pin. | ✅ Done | Overnight session 2026-07-08/09, Supabase-only (schema descriptions + method text + temperature columns), no code files touched |

Small sample, but combined with the mechanism being obviously correct (the RPC literally never returned the field before), high confidence this is fixed. | ✅ Done | Overnight session 2026-07-08/09 — Supabase migration (`match_the_library` RPC) + `lib/librarian.js` (1 code file, node build passed) |
| AA-185 | **[Continuity]** **New, found 2026-07-14 reproducing a duplicate Agent Routing drawer row John reported live** (identical "Owen is routing this to Nadia…" appearing twice, back-to-back, no intervening event). Node test (5/5, real Supabase + real Anthropic) explicitly asserts zero `onEvent` calls before the checkpoint and exactly-once dispatch on resume. **Live-verified against the real deployed dev endpoint, 3 separate live runs** (2 direct API-level, 1 full browser UI run reproducing an actual guardrail-block escalation to Sam) — every run showed exactly one checkpoint/resume cycle, zero duplicate Agent Routing rows, zero console errors. One ambiguous `durable_hops` row pair found mid-verification (Owen/Nadia, ~21:16 UTC, matching the old bug signature) is most likely a different concurrent session's traffic hitting pre-deployment code, not a residual bug — three subsequent isolated post-deployment runs (including the exact original repro question) were all clean; flagged for John rather than silently dismissed. | ✅ Done | S-ARCH-DURABLE-RESUME-02 (v6.2.31, `420f677`, pushed to dev) |
| AA-187 | **[Continuity]** **New, found 2026-07-14 root-causing a live user-reported Q&A failure (16-hop Agent Routing chain, ~2.5 min total, ended in a correct `synthesized_as_fact` guardrail block).** Direct `durable_hops` evidence, not guessed: two orphaned rows from the live repro (`8b285839…`, Marcus's `ci-answer-intent`, `hop_counter: 2`; `233c1c9d…`, Nadia's `data-analysis`, `hop_counter: 3`) were each created, patched once, and never touched again. Root cause: nested delegate calls (`request_help`/`delegate_to_agent`) inherit the outer call's shrinking deadline; when a nested call itself checkpoints, `AA-141`'s accepted design re-invokes it fresh on the outer resume rather than resuming the nested job — an "acceptable" tradeoff at the time because measured delegate-turn latencies were assumed to be 1-9s. That assumption is false for Marcus's own answer-generation turn and Nadia's `data-analysis` turn (real 12-45s LLM calls) — real work and real Anthropic spend were silently discarded and redone, the dominant contributor to the inflated hop count and latency on this question. Same fix as `AA-185` above (one root cause). **Fixed 2026-07-14 (`S-ARCH-DURABLE-RESUME-02`, v6.2.31, `420f677`):** the nested call is now never dispatched until the outer resume has a full fresh budget (pre-dispatch checkpoint persists the decision instead), so a normal-length nested turn completes in one shot with no need to checkpoint its own row. Node test's assertion 3 directly queries `durable_hops` for orphaned nested rows across a forced checkpoint/resume cycle — zero found. Live-verified the same way as `AA-185` above (3 separate live runs against the real deployed dev endpoint, zero orphaned nested rows in each isolated post-deployment window). | ✅ Done | S-ARCH-DURABLE-RESUME-02 (v6.2.31, `420f677`, pushed to dev) |

Resolution: new physically-separate table `the_reasoning` (`ARCHITECTURE.md` §19f), a third Resource Ownership Broker flavor ("Content-Owner Access," `§19e`'s registry) — Nadia and the Reasoner (`AG-20`) each write their own opinion/reasoning content directly, gated by `data_room_tag`/`uber_access` (same check `the_library` uses), zero round trip to Eleanor. Also closed a live schema gap this surfaced: §5.10's Reasoner spec assumed a `data_type` column on `knowledge_entries` that was never actually built (only `the_library` has it) — Reasoner's output retargeted to `the_reasoning` too. Promotion (opinion → fact) still goes through Eleanor unchanged, via the already-built `on_accept_intent_slug` (`AA-103`) — now the rare, deliberate exception instead of every correction's default path, which is what actually fixes the original hop-budget bug. `S-APPLE-04b`/`S-ARCH-HITL-RESUME-02` both re-scoped and shipped (`AG-34`/`AG-35`, `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`). | ✅ Done | S-ARCH-REASONING-LAYER-01a (a7ebf40) / 01b (a782907) |
## 2026-07-16 — feature-id-followup-0716 (renamed from legacy `AI-` prefix, wrongly assigned post-taxonomy)
| LOG-02 | **[Observability]** **Renamed from `AI-54` at close-out, 2026-07-16 — this is `LOG` territory under `docs/SCREEN-INVENTORY.md`'s platform-layer table (AI Audit/activity-log bugs), not a legacy `AI-` continuation; the design session that logged it missed the top-of-file "Feature ID Format" pointer.** Fabricated model claim in `AboutPanel.jsx`'s `AIStackDiagram()` — Foundation Layer text read `Models (Claude · GPT-4)`; DeepBench has never called GPT-4 anywhere in this codebase (confirmed via full grep of every `logAICall`/`logActivity` call site — only Claude for generation, OpenAI `text-embedding-3-small` for embeddings). Directly contradicted the same file's own accurate model list ~160 lines later, right under a label reading "◀ DeepBench AI Audit". Found 2026-07-16 (design session, John's direct ask to remove fabricated platform names before Apple review). **Fixed same session:** line removed. Confirmed shipped on `origin/dev` (`56e52a1`). | ✅ Done | S-AI-54-55 (v6.3.8, `56e52a1`) |
| LOG-03 | **[Observability]** **Renamed from `AI-55` at close-out, 2026-07-16** (see `LOG-02` for why). `CreateWorkOrderScreen.jsx`'s Goal Suggestion client-side `logAICall()` hardcoded `tokens: 0` on every real streaming Haiku call — `api/plan.js`'s `suggest-goal` action already received real `input_tokens`/`output_tokens` in the Anthropic SSE stream and discarded them, so this service always showed `$0.00` cost in AI Audit regardless of real usage. Found 2026-07-16, same investigation as `LOG-02`. **Fixed same session:** real usage now captured server-side and logged once via the existing shared `logActivity()` (`lib/activity-log.js`); redundant client-side `logAICall()` calls removed (same precedent as the already-fixed 'planning' duplicate in this same file). Confirmed shipped on `origin/dev` (`56e52a1`, `api/plan.js` + `CreateWorkOrderScreen.jsx`). | ✅ Done | S-AI-54-55 (v6.3.8, `56e52a1`) |
| LOG-04 | **[Observability]** **Renamed from `AI-56` at close-out, 2026-07-16** (see `LOG-02` for why). `api/plan.js`'s `suggest-goal` SSE reader (added same session as `LOG-03`) decoded and line-split each `reader.read()` chunk in isolation with no carry-over buffer across reads — any `data:` line split across two chunk boundaries was silently corrupted on both halves. Live-verified 2/2 pre-fix: real calls against the dev endpoint always came back with `input_tokens: null` (`message_start`, the first SSE event, is the one most exposed to an early chunk-boundary split), while `output_tokens`/`patterns_used` captured correctly. Root cause confirmed by direct code read (no `{stream:true}` decode option, no leftover-line buffer) — standard missing-buffer SSE parsing bug, not a transient network issue. **Fixed same session:** accumulates a running string buffer across `reader.read()` calls, splits on `\n`, holds back the last (possibly incomplete) line for the next iteration — standard buffered-SSE-parser pattern; also switched to `decoder.decode(value, {stream:true})` so a multi-byte UTF-8 character split across chunks decodes correctly too. Confirmed shipped on `origin/dev` (`22f8d6d`, `api/plan.js`). | ✅ Done | S-AI-56 (v6.3.9, `22f8d6d`) |
| LOG-08 | **[Observability]** Mechanical AI-logging coverage sweep, `scripts/check-ai-logging-coverage.js` — built 2026-07-16 after a full-session investigation found that a day of manual audits (`AA-190`, `AA-192a`/`b`, `LOG-05`–`07`) still missed real gaps (`api/plan.js`'s own core planning call had zero logging, among 6 others) because `AA-190` had explicitly scoped itself to 9 originally-found sites and nothing had mechanically re-checked coverage since. Statically scans `api/`/`lib`/`src/` for real LLM/embedding call markers (`api.anthropic.com`/`api.openai.com`/`embedContent(`) and flags any file lacking a corresponding `logActivity()`/`logAICall()` call — CRITICAL for zero-logging-attempt or undefined-reference crash risk, WARNING for a real-call-count vs. log-call-count mismatch needing human review. Verified live against the real repo to catch all 8 independently-confirmed real gaps from this session's own investigation before being considered done. **Same-day patch:** the coding session's own verification caught one real bug (missing an `embedContent(` marker, fixed before first commit); this design session's own independent re-run then caught a second — `src/hooks/useAIActivity.js` (the file that *defines* `logAICall`) was flagged as a permanent false-positive crash risk. Patched same day: strips `//` comments and the function's own definition signature before counting, which also correctly cleared a second, related false positive on `CreateWorkOrderScreen.jsx` (comment-only mentions) as a side effect. Referenced in `STANDARDS.md`'s Category M going forward. Does not fix any gap it finds — those are tracked separately, `LOG-09`/`LOG-10`/`LOG-11`. | ✅ Done | S-LOG-08-design/S-LOG-08 (v6.3.14, `773d55e`) + S-LOG-08-patch-design/S-LOG-08-patch (v6.3.15, `c930b63`) |
